“I saw him no more, nor did I hear of him again until the day when Brien O’Brien was thrown from the gates, and then I knew that he and O’Brien were the same being, and that he had really escaped and was a fourth round life of the lowest globe.

“Perhaps he will be heard of again, for he is an energetic and restless being to whom an environment is an enemy and to whom humour is an ambition and a mystery.”

---
===Chinese===
「他那些飞旋的火圈早已停了，宽宽的火焰缩成一点蓝色的微光，我眼看着它也熄灭了。他只剩光秃秃地站在三角形里，任凭我报复。他那双瞪出的、憔悴的眼睛落在我手中的霹雳上。

「『用不着那个，』他说，他说这话时倒还有几分体面，『我被那声音逮住了，我完啦。』

「这话不假，所以我没有放出霹雳。

「他的三角形那时已经在崩塌。他蹲坐下去，双臂抱住腿，头垂到膝上。我看得出他明白全完了，正拼死做最后一搏，护住自己的实体不叫它消散，他成功了——就在三角形消失前的一瞬，他没了踪影。可他不可能完全躲过那声音，那是不可能的；就算他回到了自己的行星，也必定是作为第三轮的生命，而不是他原本已达的第五轮。他得把整个进化从头再走一遍，而且还得添上沉重的一笔业障。

「此后我再没见过他，也没再听到他的消息，直到布里恩·奥布莱恩被扔出大门的那一天，我才知道他和奥布莱恩是同一个存在，他真的逃脱了，成了最低星球上的第四轮生命。

「也许他还会再传出音讯，因为他是个精力旺盛、永不安分的存在，环境于他是仇敌，幽默于他是抱负，也是谜。」

---

===Original===
“That is the end of my story,” said Art modestly.

Mac Cann regarded him indulgently from a cloud of smoke:

“It wasn’t as good as the other ones,” he remarked, “but that’s not your fault, and you’re young into the bargain.”

“He is not as young as he looks,” remarked Finaun.

“A good story has to be about ordinary things,” continued Patsy, “but there isn’t anybody could tell what your story was about.”

Billy the Music here broke in:
===Chinese===
「我的故事讲完了，」阿特谦逊地说。

麦克·坎恩从一团烟雾里宽厚地打量着他：

「不如前面几个好，」他评道，「可那不怪你，何况你还年轻。」

「他可不像看上去那么年轻，」菲瑙恩说。

「好故事得讲平常的事情，」帕齐接着说，「可你那故事讲的是什么，谁也说不上来。」

音乐家比利这时插话道：

===Original===
“The person I would have liked to hear more of is Cuchulain, for he is my own guardian angel and it’s him I’m interested in. The next time I meet him I’ll ask him questions.”

He glanced around the circle:

“Is there anybody would like to hear a tune on the concertina? I have it by my hand here, and the evening is before us.”

“You can play it for us the next time we meet,” said Patsy, “for we are all tired listening to the stories, and you are tired yourself.”

He lifted to his feet then and yawned heartily with his arms at full stretch and his fists clenched:
===Chinese===
「我还想多听听的那个人是库丘林，因为他是我自己的守护天使，我上心的就是他。下回再碰见他，我要好好问问他。」

他环视了一圈围坐的人：

「有没有人想听一曲六角手风琴？琴就在我手边，咱们还有一整个傍晚呢。」

「下回碰面再拉给我们听吧，」帕齐说，「听了一晚上故事，我们都乏了，你自己也乏了。」

说罢他站起身，伸展双臂，握紧拳头，痛痛快快打了个哈欠：

===Original===
“We had better be moving,” he continued, “for the evening is coming on and it’s twenty miles to the fair.”

They harnessed the ass.

“I’m going the opposite way to you,” said Billy the Music.

“All right,” said Patsy. “God be with you, mister.”

“God be with yourselves,” replied Billy the Music.

He tramped off then in his own direction, while Mac Cann and his companions took their road with the ass.
===Chinese===
「咱们还是动身的好，」他接着说，「天要晚了，到集市还有二十英里地呢。」

他们给毛驴套上了车。

「我跟你们不同路，我往那边去，」音乐家比利说。

「行啊，」帕齐说，「愿上帝与您同行，先生。」

「愿上帝也与你们同行，」音乐家比利答道。

随后他迈开大步，朝自己的方向去了；麦克·坎恩一行则赶着毛驴，走上了他们的路。

## Book IV: Mary Mac Cann / 第四部 玛丽·麦克·坎恩

### XXVIII / 二十八

===Original===
The search for work and food led them back, but by different paths, through Kerry, up into Connemara, and thence by stony regions to Donegal again and the rugged hills.

Their days were uneventful but they were peaceful: their nights were pleasant, and seldom did they lack for even one meal in the day. When they did so lack they passed the unwelcome hour in the silence of those to whom such an hiatus was not singular. Under Mac Cann’s captaincy the tiny band moved from meal to meal as another army would invest and sack and depart from the cities on its route.

Sometimes at night a ballad-singer would stray on their road, an angry man from whom no person had purchased songs for two days, and in return for victual this one would entertain them with his lays and recite the curses he had composed against those who did not pay the musician.

Sometimes they came on gatherings of tinkers and pedlars, tramps, and trick-men, and in the midst of these they would journey towards a fair. Uproarious nights then! Wild throats yelling at the stars and much loud trampling on the roads as the women fought and screeched, and the men howled criticism and encouragement, and came by mere criticism themselves to the battle. Paltry onslaughts these, more of word than of weapon to the fray that left some blooded noses and swollen lips as the one hour memorial of their deeds.

And again the peaceful nights, the calm stars, the quiet moon strewing her path in silver; space for the eye, the ear, and the soul; the whispering of lovely trees; the unending rustle of the grass, and the wind that came and went away and came, chanting its long rhythms or hushing its chill lullaby by the fields and the hills.

On a day when they had finished eating Finaun beckoned Caeltia and Art aside and they spoke closely together. Turning to Mac Cann and his daughter Finaun said:
===Chinese===
对活计与吃食的寻索又把他们带了回来，只是走的是另一条路：他们穿过克里，北上康尼马拉（Connemara），再从多石的地带回到多尼戈尔和那些崎岖的山丘。

他们的日子平淡，却也安宁：他们的夜晚惬意，一天里连一顿饭都不落的时候也很少。真落了的时候，他们便以一种人的静默捱过那不受欢迎的时辰——对那种人来说，这样的间断并不稀奇。在麦克·坎恩的统率之下，这支小小的队伍从一顿饭转场到另一顿饭，正如另一支军队会沿途围攻、劫掠各城，再扬长而去。

有时夜里会走岔一位民谣歌手到他们的路上来——一个满腔怒气的人，已经两天没有人向他买歌了。换取一顿吃食，这个人便用歌谣为他们助兴，还朗诵他替那些不肯付钱给乐师的人编下的咒骂。

有时他们撞上成群的补锅匠、货郎、流浪汉和骗客，便夹在这些人的中间朝一场集市赶路。那样的夜晚可真热闹！粗野的嗓子朝星星叫嚷，路上响成一片杂沓的脚步——女人们扭打尖叫，男人们吼着批评和鼓劲，吼着吼着自己也骂进了战团。这些不过是些不值一提的混战，动口的远多于动手的，打完只留下几只流血的鼻子、几张肿起的嘴唇，算作他们功业的一个时辰的纪念碑。

而后又是安宁的夜晚，静穆的星，娴静的月亮把银辉撒在她一路走过的地方；眼睛、耳朵和心灵都有了开阔的余地；可爱树木的呢喃；青草不歇的沙沙；还有那来了又去、去了又来的风，在田野和山丘旁吟着它长长的节律，或低嘘着它清寒的摇篮曲。

有一天，他们刚吃完饭，菲瑙恩把凯尔提亚和阿特招到一旁，三人低声密谈起来。随后菲瑙恩转向麦克·坎恩和他的女儿，说道：

===Original===
“We have finished what we came to do, my friends.”

Patsy nodded frowningly at him.

“What was it you came to do?”

“I came to give help to the powers,” said Finaun mildly.

“I didn’t see you doing much,” replied Patsy.

“And,” Finaun continued smilingly, “the time has come for us to go away.”
===Chinese===
「朋友们，我们此来要做的事已经做完了。」

帕齐朝他皱着眉点了点头。

「你们来是要做什么事？」

「我来是为了给众权柄帮忙，」菲瑙恩温和地说。

「我没瞧见你们帮了多少忙，」帕齐答道。

「还有，」菲瑙恩含笑接着说，「我们该动身离开的时候到了。」

===Original===
“You’re in a hurry, I suppose?”

“We are not in a great hurry, but the time has come for us to go back.”

“Very well!” said Patsy. “We aren’t so far from where we started. If we take one of the turns on the right here and bear away to the west by Cnuc-Mahon and Tober-Fola and Rath-Cormac we’ll come to the place where your things are buried, and then I suppose﻿—we can get there in three days, if that will do you?”

“That will do,” said Finaun.

During the remainder of the day he and his companions walked together talking among themselves while Mac Cann and his daughter went with the ass.

Patsy also was preoccupied all that day and she had her own thoughts; they scarcely spoke at all and the ass was bored.
===Chinese===
「你们急着走，是吧？」

「倒不算很急，只是我们回去的时候到了。」

「好啊！」帕齐说。「我们离动身的地方不算远。要是从这儿右手边的岔口拐一个，往西取道克努克-马洪（Cnuc-Mahon）、托伯-福拉（Tober-Fola）和拉斯-科马克（Rath-Cormac），就能走到埋你们东西的地方，到那时候我想——咱们三天可以到，这样行不行？」

「这样行，」菲瑙恩说。

那天剩下的时辰，他和他的同伴们走在一处自顾自交谈，麦克·坎恩则同女儿跟着毛驴走。

帕齐那一整天也是心事重重，而她有她自己的心事；他们几乎一言不发，连毛驴都闷得慌。

===Original===
At night they camped under a broken arch, the vestige of they knew not what crumbled building, and, seated around the brazier, they sunk to silence, each staring at the red glow and thinking according to their need, and it was then that Art, lifting his eyes from the brazier, looked for the first time at Mary and saw that she was beautiful.

She had been looking at him﻿—that was now her one occupation. She existed only in these surreptitious examinations. She dwelt on him broodingly as a miser burns on his gold or a mother hovers hungrily upon her infant, but he had never given her any heed. Now he was looking at her, and across the brazier their eyes communed deeply.

There was birth already between them﻿—sex was born, and something else was shaping feebly to existence. Love, that protection and cherishing, that total of life, the shy prince scarcely to be known among the teeming populations of the world, raised languidly from enchanted sleep a feeble hand.

What fire did their eyes utter! The quiet night became soundingly vocal. Winged words were around her again as in that twilight when her heart loosed its first trials of song. Though the night was about her black and calm there was dawn and sunlight in her heart, and she bathed herself deeply in the flame.
===Chinese===
夜里他们在一道残破的拱门下扎营——那是天知道什么坍塌了的建筑的遗痕。众人围火盆坐下，渐渐沉入沉默，各自凝望红光，各按各的需要想心事。就在那时，阿特的目光从火盆上抬起来，第一次望向玛丽，看见了她有多美。

她一直就在望他——这如今是她唯一的事了。她只活在这些偷偷的端详里。她栖在他身上出神，像守财奴守着他的金子，像母亲饥切地俯在她的婴儿上；可他从来没有留意过她。现在他正望着她，隔着火盆，他们的眼睛深深相通。

他们之间已经有降生在发生——性降生了，还有别的什么正软弱地成形，要来到世间。爱，那护卫与珍护，那生命的总和，这位羞怯的君王在世界熙攘的众生中几乎无从辨认；他从着了魔的睡眠里，慵慵地抬起了一只虚弱的手。

他们的眼睛吐出的是怎样的火焰啊！寂静的夜变得朗朗有声。生着翅膀的话语又环绕在她四周，正如那个暮色里她心头初试啼声的时候。尽管夜在她四周又黑又静，她心里却有黎明和阳光，她把自己深深浸在火焰里沐浴。

===Original===
And he! There is no knowing but this, that his eyes poured soft fire, enveloping, exhaustless. He surrounded her as with a sea. There she slid and fell and disappeared, to find herself again, renewed, reborn, thrilling to the embrace of those waters, wondrously alive and yet so languid that she could not move. There she rocked like a boat on the broad waves and, saving the limitless sea, there was nothing in sight. Almost he even had disappeared from her view but not from her sensation: he was an influence wide as the world, deep and steep and tremendous as all space.

They were alone. The quiet men seated beside them thinned and faded and disappeared: the night whisked from knowledge as a mounting plume of smoke that eddies and is gone: the trees and the hills tripped softly backwards and drooped away. Now they were in a world of their own, microscopic, but intense: a sphere bounded by less than the stretching of their arms: a circle of such violent movement that it was stationary as a spinning top, and her mind whirled to it, and was still from very activity. She could not think, she could not try to think, that was her stillness, but she could feel and that was her movement; she was no longer a woman but a responsiveness: she was an universal contact thrilling at every pore and point: she was surrendered and lost and captured and no longer pertained to herself.

So much can the eye do when the gathered body peers meaningly through its lens. They existed in each other: in and through each other: the three feet of distance was no longer there: it had disappeared, and they were one being swinging on league-long wings through vast spaces.

When they dropped to sleep it was merely a slipping backwards, a motion that they did not feel: they were asleep before they dropped asleep: they were asleep long before that, drugged and senseless with the strong potion of the body, stronger than aught in the world but the sharp essence of the mind that awakens all things and never permits them to be lulled again.

---

===Chinese===
而他呢！旁的无从知晓，只知他的眼睛倾注着柔软的火，包裹一切，无有穷尽。他像一片海那样环拥着她。她在其中滑落、跌坠、消没，又重新找到自己，更新了，重生了，为那些水波的拥抱而震颤；她鲜活得出奇，却又慵软得动弹不得。她在那儿像一只小船在阔浪上摇荡，除了无边的海，四望再无一物。连他也几乎从她的视野里消失了，却没从她的感觉里消失：他是一种同世界一样广的势，同全空间一样深、一样陡、一样可畏。

他们单独在一起了。坐在近旁的那些安静人影变淡了、褪散了、不见了：夜像一柱升腾的烟，打着旋儿从知觉里倏地抽走：树木和山丘轻步向后退去，垂落而逝。此刻他们在一个只属于他们自己的世界里，至微，却又至烈：一个边界还不及他们伸展手臂的球体：一个运动激烈到近乎静止的圆，就像旋转的陀螺；她的心神随之旋转，又因这极动的本身而归于宁定。她不能思考，她连试着思考也不能，这便是她的静；但她能感觉，这便是她的动；她不再是一个女人，而是一份感应：她是一次普遍的接触，在每个毛孔和每个点上震颤：她被降服了，迷失了，被掳获了，不再属于她自己。

当凝聚的身体透过眼睛的镜片意味深长地凝望时，眼睛所能做的竟有这么多。他们存在于彼此之中：在彼此之内、透过彼此而存在：那三英尺的距离不复在了：它消失了，他们成了一个生命，展开一里格又一里格长的翅膀，摇荡着穿过浩瀚的太虚。

他们坠入睡眠时，不过是向后一滑，一个他们自己都觉不出的动作：他们在睡着之前就已经睡着了：早在那之前许久就睡着了，被肉身这剂猛药麻醉了知觉——它比世上一切都强，只除了心智那锐利的精华；那精华唤醒万物，从此再不容它们被哄入沉睡。

---

===Original===
When morning dawned and the camp awakened there was some little confusion, for Mac Cann was not in the place where he had slept and they could not imagine where he had gone to.

Mary discussed his disappearance in all kinds of terms, Caeltia alone, with a downcast air, refusing to speak of it. They waited during hours for him, but he did not return, and at noon they decided to wait no longer but to go on their journey leaving him to catch on them if he was behind or hoping to gain on him if he was in advance, for their route was marked.

The angels did seem a trifle lost in his absence, and they looked with some dubiety at Mary when she took charge of their journey and of the daily provision of their food.

Food had to be gotten, and she had to discover it not alone for herself but for these other mouths. It was the first time she had been alone and, although her brows and lips were steady, her heart beat terror through her body.

For she had to do two things which she had never done and had never surmised really had to be done. She had to think, and she had to follow her thought by doing the thing she thought of. Which of these two were the more terrible she did not know, but there was no difficulty as to which she must do first, the simple orderliness of logic clamoured that she must think before she could do anything, and, so, her brain set to the painful weaving of webs too flimsy at first for any usage; but on this day she discovered where her head lay and how to use it without any assistance. She had memory to work with also, the recollection of her father’s activities, and memory is knowledge; a well-packed head and energy﻿—that is the baggage for life, it is the baggage for eternity.

She moved to the head of the ass and pulled his ear to advance. Caeltia and Finaun trod beside and they went forward. Behind came Art sniffing with the hungriest of nostrils on the sunny air, for it was five hours since they had eaten and more than three hours’ abstinence was painful to him.

===Chinese===
清晨破晓、营地醒来的时候，起了一阵小小的慌乱，因为麦克·坎恩不在他睡的地方了，他们想不出他去了哪里。

玛丽用种种的说法议论他的失踪，唯独凯尔提亚垂着头，不肯谈这件事。他们等了他好几个钟头，他没有回来；到晌午，他们决定不再等了，继续赶路——他若落在后面，就让他来追赶他们；他若走在前头，就指望能撵上他，因为他们的路线是有记号的。

他不在，天使们确实显得有几分没了主意；当玛丽接管了他们的行程和每日吃食的筹办时，他们带几分迟疑地望着她。

吃食总得弄到，而且她不光要为自己，还要为这几张别的嘴去寻摸。这是她头一回独自担当：虽然她的眉头和嘴唇都很镇定，她的心却把恐怖一下下捶打进她的身体。

因为她得做两件她从未做过、也从没真正想到过必须去做的事。她得思考，她还得起而行动，把她想到的那件事做出来。这两件哪一件更可怕，她不知道；但先做哪一件却毫无疑义，逻辑那简单明了的秩序大声疾呼：她必须先思考，然后才能有所行动。于是她的头脑着手痛苦地编织起蛛网，起初织得太薄，什么用处也当不起；可就在这一天，她发现了自己的脑袋长在哪里，学会了不靠任何帮助去使用它。她还有记忆可用——对父亲种种作为的追忆，而记忆就是知识；一只装得扎实的脑袋，加上精力——这就是人生的行囊，也是永恒的行囊。

她走到毛驴头前，拽拽它的耳朵让它前进。凯尔提亚和菲瑙恩在旁边踏步随行，队伍向前去了。后面跟着阿特，用最饥饿的鼻孔嗅着晴暖的空气——他们已经五个钟头没吃东西了，而三个多钟头的禁食对他来说是痛苦的。

### XXIX / 二十九

===Original===
She did get food. She nourished her three children sumptuously, but she made them help her to get it.

She looked at Finaun’s high nose, his sweeping beard, his air as of a good child well matured, and she sent it to the market:

“One must eat,” said she.

When they came to a house by the roadside she ordered Finaun to the door to ask for bread; he got it too and had eaten but the slowest mouthful when she seized it from him and stocked it for the common good.
===Chinese===
吃食她当真弄到了。她把她那三个孩子养得丰足体面，却也逼着他们帮她一起去弄。

她端详菲瑙恩的高鼻梁、飘洒的大胡子和那副好孩子长大成人了的气派，便把他派去集市：

「人总得吃饭，」她说。

走到路边一户人家，她就吩咐菲瑙恩上门讨面包；他真讨到了，才吃了最慢的一口，她就从他手里夺了过去，存起来充作公用。

===Original===
She charged Caeltia through the open door of a cottage, and his expedition was famous for eight hours afterwards.

She performed feats herself in a fowl house and a cattle pen, but she did not issue any commands to Art except at the falling-to, when he obeyed adequately.

She recalled the deeds of her father in many predicaments, and for the first time she really understood his ceaseless skill and activity. She found too that she could recollect his tactics, beside which her own were but childish blunderings, and, with that memory she mended her hand, and life became the orderly progression which everybody expects it to be.

That night by the glow of the brazier she rested a mind that had never been weary before, and she craved for the presence of her father that she might gain from him the praise which her present companions did not know was due to her.

---

===Chinese===
她催着凯尔提亚从一座小屋敞开的门里冲进去，他这一役在之后八个钟头里一直被人传颂。

她自己也曾在鸡舍和牛栏里建过功勋；但对阿特，除了开饭的时候，她从不下任何命令——开饭时他倒是听从得挺像样。

她回想起父亲在许多窘境里的作为，头一回真正懂得了他那不歇的机变与勤快。她还发现自己记得住他的种种手法——相比之下，她自己的那些不过是小孩子式的瞎折腾。凭着这份记忆，她的手段长进了，日子也变成了人人指望的那种有条不紊的进程。

那天夜里，就着火盆的红光，她让一颗从来不曾疲倦过的心歇了下来。她渴望父亲就在身边，好从他那里讨得一句夸奖——她眼前的这些同伴，并不知道这一句夸奖是她应得的。

---

===Original===
“Two days more,” said her heart, communicating to her bitterly as they proceeded on the morrow morning, but she banished the thought and set to her plots and plans. She banished it, but it clung with her, vague and weighty as a nightmare, and when she looked backwards on the road Art’s eyes were looking into hers with a quietness that almost drove her mad. She could not understand him.

They had never spoken to each other; not once had they spoken directly since that night when he stepped into the glow of the brazier. At first she had fled from him in a fear which was all shyness and wildness, and so an overlooking habit had been formed between them which he had never sought to break, and which she did not know how to put an end to.

“Two days!” said her heart again, pealing it to her through her webs, and again she exiled her heart, and could feel its wailing when she could hear it no longer.

---

===Chinese===
「还剩两天了，」第二天早晨他们上路时，她的心苦涩地向她通报；可她把这念头赶开，埋头去搞她的盘算和计划。她赶开了它，它却缠着她不走，像一场梦魇似的又模糊又沉重；而当她回头望路时，阿特的眼睛正静静望进她的眼睛里，那份安静几乎把她逼疯。她看不透他。

他们从来没有交谈过；自打那一夜他踏进火盆的红光里起，他们连一句正面的招呼都不曾有过。起初她躲着他，那躲避里全是羞怯和仓皇；于是两人之间养成了一种彼此只当没看见的习惯——他从未想打破它，她也不知道该怎样了结它。

「两天！」她的心又说了一遍，穿过她织的种种网向她敲响；她又把这颗心放逐了，等到再也听不见它的声音时，还能觉出它的哀号。

---

===Original===
They stopped for the midday meal; bread and potatoes and a morsel of cheese; the fare was plentiful, and from a stream near by good water washed it down.

The reins of the donkey were thrown across the limb of a tree, and he had liberty to browse in a circle. He also had his drink from the running stream, and was glad of it.

As they sat three people marched the road behind them; they saw these people, and studied their advance.

A talkative, a disorderly advance it was. An advance that halted every few paces for parley, and moved on again like a battle.

Two men and a woman were in that party, and it did seem that they were fighting every inch of their way. Certainly, they were laughing also, for a harsh peal came creaking up the road, and came again. Once the laugh broke abruptly on its gruff note as though a hand had pounded into its middle. Then the party parleyed again and moved again.

What they said could not be distinguished, but the rumour of their conversation might have been heard across the world. They bawled and screamed, and always through the tumult came the gruff hoot of laughter.
===Chinese===
他们停下来吃晌午饭：面包、土豆，还有一小块干酪；饭食很充裕，旁边一条溪里淌来的好水把它送下肚去。

毛驴的缰绳搭上了一根树杈，它可以自由自在地在圈子里啃草。它也从那道奔流的小溪里喝了水，喝得心满意足。

他们正坐着，三个人沿着他们后面的路走了过来；他们望见了这几个人，留意起他们的行进。

那是一场吵吵嚷嚷、乱乱糟糟的行进。走不上几步便停下谈判，再又开拔，像一场战役。

那一伙里是两个男人和一个女人，看那样子简直是一寸一寸打着过来的。当然，他们也在笑，因为一路上嘎嘎地滚过来一阵刺耳的大笑，又是一阵。有一回那笑声在粗厉的调子上戛然而止，像有一只拳头砸进了它的半腰。随后这一伙又谈起来，又动身。

他们说的什么听不真切，可他们谈话的声浪隔着半个世界都未必听不见。他们又叫又嚷，而那阵粗嘎的大笑始终穿透喧腾，一阵阵地滚出来。

===Original===
Said Caeltia:

“Do you know these people?”

“The woman is Eileen Ni Cooley,” replied Mary, “for I know her walk, but I don’t know the shape of the men.”

Caeltia laughed quietly to himself.

“The taller of these men,” said he, “is the seraph Cuchulain, the other man is that Brien O’Brien we were telling you of.”

Mary’s face flamed, but she made no remark.
===Chinese===
凯尔提亚说道：

「你认得这几个人吗？」

「那女人是艾琳·尼·库利，」玛丽答道，「因为我认得她走路的样子，可那两个男人的身形我认不得。」

凯尔提亚暗自轻轻笑了。

「那两个男人里高些的那个，」他说，「是炽天使库丘林；另一个就是我们对你们讲过的那个布里恩·奥布莱恩。」

玛丽的脸腾地红了，但她什么也没说。

===Original===
In a few minutes these people drew near.

Eileen Ni Cooley was dishevelled. Her shawl hung only from one shoulder and there were holes in it, her dress was tattered, and a long wisp of red hair streamed behind her like a flame. Her face was red also, and her eyes were anxious as they roved from one to the other.

She came directly to the girl and sat beside her; young Cuchulain set himself down beside Art, but Brien O’Brien stood a few paces distant with his fists thrust in his pockets and he chewing strongly on tobacco. Every now and then he growled a harsh creak of a laugh and then covered it ostentatiously with his hand.

“God be with you, Mary Ni Cahan,” said Eileen Ni Cooley, and she twisted up her flying hair and arranged her shawl.

“What’s wrong with you?” said Mary.

“Where’s your father?” said Eileen.
===Chinese===
不出几分钟，这几个人走近了。

艾琳·尼·库利蓬头散发。她的披巾只挂在一个肩膀上，上面还破了几个洞；裙子褴褛，一长绺红头发像一道火苗似的飘在身后。她的脸也红着，一双眼睛不安地从一个人身上溜到另一个人身上。

她径直走到姑娘跟前，在她身边坐下；年轻的库丘林在阿特身旁落座；布里恩·奥布莱恩却站在几步开外，两只拳头插在口袋里，腮帮子有力地嚼着烟叶。他不时喉咙里滚出一声粗嘎发涩的笑，随即又故意拿手把嘴一捂。

「上帝与你同在，玛丽·尼·卡汉（Mary Ni Cahan），」艾琳·尼·库利说着，把飘散的头发拧起来盘好，理了理披巾。

「你怎么弄成这样？」玛丽说。

「你父亲呢？」艾琳说。

===Original===
“I don’t know where he is. When we lifted from sleep a morning ago he wasn’t in his place, and we haven’t seen him since that time.”

“What am I going to do at all?” said Eileen in a low voice. “These men have me tormented the way I don’t know how to manage.”

“What could my father do?” said Mary sternly, “and you playing tricks on him since the day you were born.”

“That’s between myself and him,” replied Eileen, “and it doesn’t matter at all. I wanted your father to beat O’Brien for me, for he won’t leave me alone day or night, and I can’t get away from him.”

Mary leaned to her whispering:

“My father couldn’t beat that man, for I saw the two of them fighting on the Donnybrook Road, and he had no chance against him.”
===Chinese===
「我不知道他在哪儿。一天早晨我们睡醒起来，他就不在他睡的地方了，打那以后我们再没见过他。」

「我到底该怎么办呢？」艾琳低声说。「这两个男人把我折磨得没了办法。」

「我父亲又能做什么？」玛丽厉声说，「你打出生那天起就捉弄他。」

「那是我跟他之间的事，」艾琳答道，「根本不碍事。我是想要你父亲替我揍奥布莱恩一顿，他白天黑夜缠着我不放，我甩不掉他。」

玛丽凑近她，悄声说：

「我父亲打不过那个人，我见过他们俩在唐尼布鲁克大道上打架，他一点胜算都没有。」

===Original===
“He could beat him, indeed,” said Eileen indignantly, “and I’d give him good help myself.”

“If my father owes you anything,” said Mary, “I’m ready to pay it for him, so let us both rise against the man, and maybe the pair of us would make him fly.”

Eileen stared at her.

“I hit him once,” continued Mary, “and I would like well to hit him again; my people here would keep his friend from joining against us.”

The blue eyes of Eileen Ni Cooley shone with contentment; she slipped the shawl from her shoulders and let it drop to the ground.

“We’ll do that, Mary,” said she, “and let us do it now.”
===Chinese===
「他就是打得过，」艾琳愤愤地说，「我自己还会好好帮衬他一手呢。」

「要是我父亲欠了你什么，」玛丽说，「我乐意替他还上。咱们俩一齐起来对付这家伙，兴许两个人能打得他飞起来。」

艾琳直勾勾盯着她。

「我打过他一回，」玛丽接着说，「我很想再打他一回；我这几个人会拦住他那位朋友，不让他过来帮拳。」

艾琳·尼·库利的蓝眼睛发亮了，透着称心如意；她把披巾从肩头一抖，任它落在地上。

「就这么办，玛丽，」她说，「咱们现在就动手。」

===Original===
So the women lifted to their feet and they walked towards Brien O’Brien, and suddenly they leaped on him like a pair of panthers, and they leaped so suddenly that he went down against the road with a great bump. But he did not stay down.

He rose after one dumbfounded moment, and he played with the pair of them the way a conjurer would play with two balls, so that the breath went out of their bodies, and they had to sit down or suffocate.

“That’s the kind of man he is,” panted Eileen.

“Very well!” said Mary fiercely, “we’ll try him again in a minute.”

The camp was in confusion, and from that confusion Art leaped towards Brien O’Brien, but the seraph Cuchulain leaped and outleaped Art, and set himself bristling by the elbow of his friend; then Caeltia, with his face shining happily, tiptoed forward and ranged with Art against these two, but Finaun went quicker than they all; he leaped between the couples, and there was not a man of the four dared move against his hand.

In a second that storm blew itself out, and they returned to their seats smiling foolishly.
===Chinese===
于是两个女人站起身，朝布里恩·奥布莱恩走去，猛然间像一对豹子似的扑到他身上——扑得那样突然，他咚的一声摔在路上。可他没有躺在地上不起来。

他愣了一瞬便爬起来，拿她们两个耍弄起来，就像变戏法的耍弄两颗球，直弄得她们两个气都喘不上来，不坐下就得憋死。

「他就是这么一号人物，」艾琳喘着气说。

「好啊！」玛丽凶狠地说，「过一分钟咱们再试试他。」

营地乱作一团，就在这乱局里，阿特朝布里恩·奥布莱恩扑过去；可炽天使库丘林扑得比他还快，一步抢在他前头，浑身倒竖地守住自己朋友的手肘边；接着凯尔提亚满面欢容，踮着脚抢上前去，同阿特站作一线对付那两个；而菲瑙恩比他们都快——他一跃跳到两对人中间，那四个人没有一个敢对他的手掌动一根指头。

转眼间那场风暴便自行平息，众人各自回到座位上，傻乎乎地笑着。

===Original===
“Let the women be quiet,” said Brien O’Brien harshly.

He also seated himself, with his back touching against the donkey’s legs.

The ass had finished eating and drinking, and was now searching the horizon with the intent eye of one who does not see anything, but only looks on the world without in order to focus steadily the world within.

Brien O’Brien stared with a new interest at Finaun, and revolved his quid. Said he to Cuchulain:

“Would the old lad be able to treat us the way Rhadamanthus did, do you think?”

“He could do that,” laughed Cuchulain, “and he could do it easily.”
===Chinese===
「叫女人们安静点，」布里恩·奥布莱恩粗声粗气地说。

他也坐下了，背抵着毛驴的腿。

毛驴吃完了也喝完了，正用一种专注的眼神搜索着地平线——那种眼神什么也没看，只是望着外部的世界，好让内心的世界稳稳地对上焦。

布里恩·奥布莱恩带着新的兴味盯住菲瑙恩，嘴里转动着那团烟叶。他对库丘林说：

「你说，那老家伙能不能像拉达曼提斯那样收拾咱们一顿？」

「他能，」库丘林笑道，「而且轻而易举。」

===Original===
O’Brien moved the quid to the other side of his jaw.

“If he slung us out of this place we wouldn’t know where we might land,” said he.

“That is so,” replied Cuchulain, thrusting a sleek curl between his teeth. “I don’t know these regions, and I don’t know where we might land, or if we would ever land. Only for that I would go against him,” and he waggled his finger comically at Finaun.

Art commenced to snigger and Finaun laughed heartily, but Caeltia eyed Cuchulain so menacingly that the seraph kept a quiet regard on him for the rest of the day.

Peace was restored, and while they were revolving peace and wondering how to express it, Patsy Mac Cann came on them from a side path that ran narrowly between small hills.

===Chinese===
奥布莱恩把那团烟叶挪到下巴的另一边。

「要是他把咱们从这地方甩出去，」他说，「咱们可不知道会落在哪儿。」

「正是这话，」库丘林答道，把一绺油亮的鬈发塞进牙齿间。「这地方我不熟，不知道咱们会落在哪儿，也不知道到底落不落得下来。若不是为这一层，我早就跟他干了。」说着他朝菲瑙恩滑稽地晃了晃手指。

阿特哧哧地笑起来，菲瑙恩放声大笑；凯尔提亚却朝库丘林逼视得那样凶，害得这位炽天使在那天剩下的时间里一直悄悄提防着他。

太平恢复了。众人正转着太平的念头、琢磨着该怎样把它说出口，帕齐·麦克·坎恩从小山之间一条窄窄的岔路上，朝他们走来了。

### XXX / 三十

===Original===
When Mac Cann saw the visitors he halted for an instant and then came forward very slowly, with his head on one side and his thumb rasping steadily on his chin.

He was staring at Brien O’Brien, and as he stared he bristled like a dog.

“It’s the man himself,” said he, “the man that stole my clothes.”

O’Brien peeped upwards at him but did not move.

“Sit down and hold your prate,” said he, “or I’ll steal your life.”

Mac Cann would have thrown himself on his enemy, but at that moment he caught sight of Eileen Ni Cooley and her face drove the other out of his head.
===Chinese===
麦克·坎恩望见这些来客，怔了一瞬，随后极慢地上前来，头歪在一边，大拇指在下巴上不停地刮蹭。

他盯住的是布里恩·奥布莱恩，一边盯，一边像狗一样毛都炸了起来。

「正是这家伙本人，」他说，「偷我衣服的就是他。」

奥布莱恩眼睛朝上瞟了他一眼，没有动。

「坐下，闭上你的嘴，」他说，「不然我把你的命也偷走。」

麦克·坎恩本要朝仇人扑过去，可就在那一瞬他望见了艾琳·尼·库利，她的脸把那人从他脑子里赶了出去。

===Original===
He stared.

“It’s yourself!” said he.

“It is me, sure enough, Padraig.”

“You’ll be going away in a minute, I suppose,” said he grimly.

He sat on the grass and there was peace once more. He was sitting beside O’Brien, and the ass was still thinking deeply with his hocks touching against their shoulder blades.

When he seated himself they were all silent, for, in face of everything, Mac Cann took the lead, and they waited for him to speak.
===Chinese===
他直愣愣望着。

「是你本人哪！」他说。

「可不是我嘛，帕德里克（Padraig）。」

「我猜你过一分钟就要走了，」他冷冷地说。

他在草地上坐下，太平又恢复了。他坐在奥布莱恩旁边，毛驴还在深深地思索，两只后蹄的飞节抵着他们的肩胛。

他一落座，众人便都沉默了，因为不管怎么说，麦克·坎恩是拿主的，大家都等他开口。

===Original===
O’Brien was looking at him sideways with a grin on his hard jaw. He creaked out a little laugh and then covered it up with his hand as one who was abashed, but Mac Cann paid no attention to him.

His attention was on Eileen Ni Cooley.

“You’re a great woman,” said he, “and you’re full of fun surely.”

“I’m everything you like to call me,” replied Eileen.

“Which of the men are you with this time, or are you travelling with the pair of them?”

“I don’t want either of them, Padraig, but I can’t get away from them anyhow. They won’t let me go my own road, and they’re marching at my elbows for two days and two nights, cursing and kicking and making a noise every step of the way.”
===Chinese===
奥布莱恩斜眼看着他，僵硬的下巴上挂着一副嬉笑。他嘎地笑出一小声，又像害臊的人那样拿手捂住，麦克·坎恩却全然不理会他。

他的心思全在艾琳·尼·库利身上。

「你是个了不起的女人，」他说，「浑身都是快活劲儿。」

「你乐意叫我什么都行，」艾琳答道。

「这一回你跟着的是两个男人里的哪一个，还是干脆两个一道走？」

「哪一个我都不要，帕德里克，可我怎么也甩不掉他们。他们不放我走自己的路，跟着我的胳膊肘行军了两天两夜，一路咒骂、一路踢打、一路吵闹，一步都不消停。」

===Original===
“They’re doing that!” said Patsy.

“They are doing that, Padraig. It’s O’Brien is the worst, for the other fellow is only helping him and doesn’t care for me at all. Catching me they do be, and holding me.﻿ ﻿…”

“Aye!” said Patsy.

“I can’t get away from O’Brien,” said she, “and I thought that if I could find yourself﻿—”

“You were looking for me?”

“I was looking for you this time, Padraig.”
===Chinese===
「他们竟这样！」帕齐说。

「他们就是这样，帕德里克。数奥布莱恩最凶，那一个不过是帮他，压根儿不把我放在心上。他们老是逮我，抱住我。……」

「唉！」帕齐说。

「我摆脱不了奥布莱恩，」她说，「我想着要是能找到你本人——」

「你在找我？」

「这一回我就是在找你，帕德里克。」

===Original===
“Aye!” said Patsy, and he turned a black eye on Brien O’Brien, and his eye looked like a little, hard ball of stone.

“You’ll be left alone from this day out,” said Patsy.

“Mind yourself!” growled Brien O’Brien. “Mind yourself, my hardy man, or you’ll waken up among the spooks.”

Patsy held him with that solid eye.

“Spooks!” said he, and suddenly he rolled on top of Brien O’Brien, his left hand grabbing at the throat, his right fist jabbing viciously with packed knuckles.

Down went Brien O’Brien’s head and up went his heels; then he gave a mighty wriggle and started to come up, his hands threshing like the wings of a mill. As he came up they rolled, and now Mac Cann was below; but Brien O’Brien’s head had disturbed the donkey, and, without emerging from cogitation, the ass let his two heels fly at the enemy of thought behind him; Patsy saw for an instant the white flash of those little hoofs across his face, but Brien of the O’Brien nation took them full on his forehead and his brows crackled in like the shell of an egg; he relaxed, he sagged, he drooped and huddled limply to Patsy’s bosom, and for three seconds Mac Cann lay quietly beneath him, captured by astonishment.
===Chinese===
「唉！」帕齐说，随即把一只乌黑的眼睛转向布里恩·奥布莱恩，那只眼睛像一颗又小又硬的石球。

「打今天起，就放你一个人清净了。」帕齐说。

「你自己当心！」布里恩·奥布莱恩吼道。「当心点，我的硬汉，不然你会在鬼魂堆里醒来。」

帕齐拿那颗结实的眼睛钉住了他。

「鬼魂！」他说着，猛然翻上布里恩·奥布莱恩的身子，左手抓他的喉咙，右拳攥得结结实实，恶狠狠地连戳带捣。

布里恩·奥布莱恩的头栽了下去，脚跟翘了上来；接着他猛力一挣，开始往上翻，两只手像风车的翅膀一样扑打。他往上翻时两人滚作一团，这回麦克·坎恩压在了下面；可布里恩·奥布莱恩的脑袋搅扰了毛驴，那驴子没有从沉思里出来，扬起两只后蹄，朝身后那个思想的敌人踢去；帕齐有那么一瞬看见那两只小蹄子白光一闪掠过自己的脸，而奥布莱恩氏族的布里恩却结结实实挨在了脑门上，眉骨咔啦一声塌了进去，像一只蛋壳；他松了，塌了，垂了下来，软软地缩在帕齐的胸口；有三个秒钟，麦克·坎恩静静地躺在他底下，被惊愕俘获了。

===Original===
The donkey had again related the infinity without to the eternity within, and his little hoofs were as peaceful as his mild eye.

Mac Cann tugged himself from beneath that weighty carcass and came to his feet.

Mary and Eileen were both sitting rigid, with arms at full stretch and their fingers tipping straitly on the ground, while their round eyes were wide in an unwinking stare.

Caeltia was on his feet and was crouching at an equally crouching Cuchulain. Patsy saw the curl jerking as the lips of the seraph laughed.
===Chinese===
毛驴又一次把外在的无限同内在的永恒联系了起来，它的小蹄子同它温驯的眼睛一样安详。

麦克·坎恩从那具沉重的躯壳底下挣出来，站起了身。

玛丽和艾琳都直挺挺地坐着，双臂撑直，手指笔直地点在地上，一双双圆眼睛瞪得溜圆，一眨不眨。

凯尔提亚站了起来，正对着同样蹲伏着的库丘林蹲伏。帕齐看见炽天使笑起来时那绺鬈发在一跳一跳。

===Original===
Art was frozen on one knee in the mid act of rising, and Finaun was combing his beard while he looked fixedly at Eileen Ni Cooley.

Twenty seconds only had elapsed since Mac Cann rolled sideways on Brien O’Brien.

The seraph Cuchulain was staring under Caeltia’s arm. He blew the golden curl from his lips and sounded a laugh that was like the ringing of silver bells.

“What will Rhadamanthus say this time?” quoth he, and with that he turned and tripped happily down the road and away.

---

===Chinese===
阿特僵在单膝上，正起半截身；菲瑙恩一边梳理胡子，一边目不转睛地望着艾琳·尼·库利。

从麦克·坎恩翻身扑向布里恩·奥布莱恩算起，统共才过了二十秒。

炽天使库丘林正从凯尔提亚的腋下张望。他把那绺金发从唇边吹开，发出一声笑，像银铃的叮当。

「这一回拉达曼提斯又该说什么呢？」他说完，转身踏着轻快的步子沿路走远了。

---

===Original===
Mac Cann regarded the corpse.

“We had better bury the man,” said he gloomily.

He took a short spade from the cart, and with it he made a hole in the roadside.

They laid Brien O’Brien in that hole.

“Wait for a minute,” said Mac Cann. “It’s not decent to send him off that way.”
===Chinese===
麦克·坎恩端详着那具尸体。

「咱们最好把这个人埋了。」他郁郁地说。

他从大篷车上取下一把短锹，在路边挖出一个坑。

他们把布里恩·奥布莱恩放进那个坑里。

「等一下，」麦克·坎恩说，「就这么打发他上路，不像样子。」

===Original===
He pushed a hand into his pocket and pulled it out again with money in it.

“He should have something with himself and he taking the long journey.”

He lifted O’Brien’s clenched fist, forced it open, and put a silver threepenny piece into it; then he tightened the pale hand again and folded it with the other on his breast.

They wrapped a newspaper about his face, and they threw the clay over Brien of the O’Brien nation and stamped it down well with their feet, and as they left him the twilight stole over the land, and a broad star looked peacefully down through the grey distances.

===Chinese===
他把一只手伸进口袋，再掏出来时手里有了钱。

「他要走这么远的路，身边总得带点什么。」

他抬起奥布莱恩攥紧的拳头，掰开它，放进一枚三便士银币；再把那只苍白的手指拢紧，同另一只手一起交叠在他胸前。

他们拿一张报纸裹住他的脸，把泥土盖上奥布莱恩氏族的布里恩，又用脚把土踩得严严实实。他们离开他的时候，暮色悄悄漫过了大地，一颗大星穿过灰蒙蒙的远处，安安静静地俯望着人间。

### XXXI / 三十一

===Original===
They walked through the evening.

Dusk had fallen and in the drowsy half-lights the world stretched itself in peacefulness.

They had come to a flat country that whispered in grass; there were no more of the little hills that roll and fall and roll; there were scarcely any trees; here and there in great space a beech swung its slow boughs and made a quiet noise in the stillness; here and there a stiff tree lifted its lonely greenness, and around it the vast horizon stretched away and away to sightlessness.

There was silence here, there was deep silence, and over all the dusk drowsed and folded and increased.

With what slow veils the darkness deepened! the gentle weaver spun her thin webs and drooped soft coverings from the sky to the clay; momently the stars came flashing their tiny signals, gathering their bright hosts by lonely clusters, and one thin sickle of the moon grew from a cloud and stood distantly as a sign of gold.

But the quiet beauty of the heavens and the quiet falling to sleep of the earth had for this night no effect on one of our travellers.
===Chinese===
他们穿过黄昏走着路。

暮色已经落下，在令人欲睡的微明里，世界舒展着身子，一片安宁。

他们走到了一片平坦的原野，四下里是青草的低语；再没有那些滚动着、起伏着、又滚动着的小山了；树也几乎不见；开阔的天地间偶或有一棵山毛榉缓缓摇着枝条，在寂静里弄出一点轻响；偶或有一棵挺立的树举起它孤单的绿冠，而在它四周，辽阔的地平线伸了又伸，一直伸到目力不见的地方。

这里是寂静，是深深的寂静，而暮色笼着一切，昏昏沉沉，一层叠一层，愈积愈浓。

黑暗披着多么缓慢的面纱一步步加深啊！温柔的织者纺着她细薄的网，把柔软的覆盖从天上垂到泥土；星星不时闪出它们细小的信号，按着孤单的星团聚拢它们明亮的队伍，一弯细细的月镰从云里长出来，远远地立着，像一枚黄金的记号。

可是这天夜里，天空静谧的美和大地上安静的入睡，对我们的一位旅人却全不起作用。

===Original===
Mac Cann was ill at ease. He was moody and irritable, and he moved from Eileen Ni Cooley to his daughter and back again to Eileen Ni Cooley and could not content himself with either of them.

The angels were treading at the rear of the cart talking among themselves; the slow drone of their voices drifted up the road, and from this murmuring the words God and Beauty and Love would detach themselves and sing recurringly on the air like incantations.

Eileen Ni Cooley trod on the left side of the ass. She trod like a featureless shade; her shawl wrapped blottingly about her face and her mind moving within herself and for herself.

Mac Cann and his daughter went together by the right side of the donkey, and, as he looked constantly at his daughter, his eyes were furtive and cunning.

He tapped her elbow.

“Mary,” he whispered, “I want to talk to you.”
===Chinese===
麦克·坎恩心神不宁。他忽而阴沉忽而烦躁，从艾琳·尼·库利身边挪到女儿身边，又从女儿身边挪回艾琳·尼·库利身边，对哪一个都安不下心来。

天使们跟在大篷车后面走着，彼此交谈；他们低缓的嗡嗡声顺着路漂上来，从这片絮语里，「上帝」「美」和「爱」这几个词不时脱出来，像念咒一样在空中反复吟唱。

艾琳·尼·库利走在毛驴左边。她走得像一道没有眉目的影子；披巾懵懵地裹着脸，心思只在自身之内、只为自身而动。

麦克·坎恩和女儿并排走在毛驴右边；他不住地瞟女儿，那双眼睛鬼祟而狡黠。

他碰了碰她的胳膊肘。

「玛丽，」他悄声说，「我想跟你谈谈。」

===Original===
She replied in a voice that was low from his contact.

“I want to talk to yourself,” said she.

“What do you want to say?”

“I want to know where you got the money that I saw in your hand when you buried the man?”

“That’s what I’m going to tell you about,” he whispered. “Be listening to me now and don’t make any noise.”

“I’m listening to you,” said Mary.
===Chinese===
她回话的声音，因他的触碰而低了下去。

「我也正想跟你本人谈谈。」她说。

「你要说什么？」

「我想知道，你埋那个人的时候，我在你手里看见的钱是哪儿来的？」

「我正要跟你说这个，」他悄声道，「这会儿好好听着，别弄出声响。」

「我听着呢。」玛丽说。

===Original===
“What have we got to do with these lads behind us?” said Patsy urgently. “They are nothing to us at all, and I’m tired of them.”

“There’s a thing to say!” quoth she.

“This is what we’ll do. Tonight we won’t unyoke the ass, and when they are well asleep we’ll walk quietly off with ourselves and leave them there. Eileen Ni Cooley will come with us and in the morning we’ll be distant.”

“I won’t do that,” said Mary.

He darted at her a sparkle of rage.

“You’ll do what I say, you strap, or it’ll be the worse for you!” said his violent whisper.
===Chinese===
「咱们跟后面这几个家伙有什么相干？」帕齐急切地说。「他们于咱们什么都不是，我都腻烦他们了。」

「这话说的！」她道。

「咱们这么办：今天夜里不解驴套，等他们睡熟了，咱们自己悄悄走掉，把他们撂在那儿。艾琳·尼·库利会跟咱们走，到早晨咱们就远了。」

「我不干。」玛丽说。

他朝她迸出一星怒火。

「照我说的做，你这贱丫头，不然有你好受的！」他的耳语凶暴起来。

===Original===
“I won’t do that,” she hissed, “and I tell you I won’t.”

“By the living Jingo﻿ ﻿… !” said Patsy.

She came at him whispering with equal fierceness.

“What have you done on the men?” said she. “What did you do on them that you want to run away from them in the night?”

“Keep your tongue in your teeth, you﻿—!”

“Where were you for a day and a half? Where did you get the money from that I saw in your hand when you buried the man?”
===Chinese===
「我不干，」她咬着牙低嘶，「我告诉你，我不干。」

「我的老天爷呀……！」帕齐说。

她也带着同样的凶劲朝他逼过去低语。

「你对他们做了什么？」她说。「你对他们做了什么，要半夜里从他们身边逃走？」

「把舌头收好，你——！」

「你那一天半上哪儿去了？你埋那人时我在你手里看见的钱，是打哪儿来的？」

===Original===
Patsy composed himself with difficulty; he licked his dry lips.

“There’s no fooling you, alannah, and I’ll tell you the truth.”

He glanced cautiously to where the others were coming deep in talk.

“This is what I did. I went to that place by Ard-Martin where we buried the things, and I dug them up.”

“Oh!” said Mary.

“I dug them up, and I took them away, and I sold them to a man for money.”
===Chinese===
帕齐费了好大劲才定下神来；他舔了舔发干的嘴唇。

「骗不过你，乖女儿，那我就跟你说实话。」

他小心地瞟了一眼走在后面、谈得入神的那几位。

「我干的是这个：我到阿德-马丁（Ard-Martin）旁边我们埋东西的那个地方去，把它们挖了出来。」

「哦！」玛丽说。

「我把它们挖出来，带走了，卖给了一个人，换了钱。」

===Original===
“Oh!” said Mary.

“They’re sold, do you hear? And there’s no going back on it; so do what I tell you about the ass this night and we’ll take our own road from now on.”

“I won’t do it,” whispered Mary, and she was almost speechless with rage.

Mac Cann thrust his face close to hers grinning like a madman.

“You won’t do it!” said he. “What will you do then against your father?”

“I’ll go on to the place with the men,” she stammered.
===Chinese===
「哦！」玛丽说。

「卖都卖了，听见没有？这事翻不回去；照我今晚吩咐你的对付那头驴，往后咱们就走咱们自己的路。」

「我不干。」玛丽低声说，气得几乎说不出话来。

麦克·坎恩把脸凑到她跟前，咧着嘴笑得像个疯子。

「你不干！」他说。「那你能拿你父亲怎么样？」

「我要同这几个人一起走到那个地方去。」她结结巴巴地说。

===Original===
“You’ll come with me this night.”

“I’ll not go,” said she harshly.

“You’ll come with me this night,” said he.

“I’ll not go,” she screamed at him.

At the sound of her scream everybody came running to them.

“Is there anything wrong?” said Art.
===Chinese===
「你今晚跟我走。」

「我不去。」她生硬地说。

「你今晚跟我走。」他说。

「我不去！」她冲他尖叫起来。

听见她的尖叫，大家都朝他们跑来。

「出什么事了吗？」阿特说。

===Original===
“She’s only laughing at a joke I told her,” said Patsy. “Make that ass go on, Mary a grah, for it’s walking as if it was going asleep.”

Caeltia was looking at Mac Cann so fixedly, with such a severe gravity of eye, that the blood of the man turned to water and he could scarcely hold himself upright. For the first time in his life Mac Cann knew what fear was.

“Tomorrow,” said Caeltia, “we will be going away from you, let us be peaceful then for our last night together.”

“Aye,” said Patsy, “let us be comfortable for this night of all nights.”

He turned away, and with a great effort at carelessness he moved to the donkey’s head.

“Come on, Mary,” said he.
===Chinese===
「她不过是听了我讲的笑话在笑，」帕齐说。「叫那头驴往前走啊，玛丽，a grah（盖尔语：亲爱的），它走得像是快要睡着了。」

凯尔提亚那样目不转睛地望着麦克·坎恩，眼神那样严厉庄重，这人的血都化成了水，几乎站都站不住。麦克·坎恩平生头一回知道了什么叫害怕。

「明天，」凯尔提亚说，「我们就要离开你们了，让我们和和气气地过这最后一夜吧。」

「唉，」帕齐说，「千夜万夜，就数这一夜要过得舒坦。」

他转过身，装出满不在乎的样子，费了好大劲才走到毛驴头前。

「来吧，玛丽。」他说。

===Original===
Eileen Ni Cooley trod beside him for a moment.

“What’s wrong with you, Padraig?” said she.

“Nothing at all, Eileen, just leave me alone for a minute for I want to talk to the girl.”

“You can count on me for anything, Padraig.”

“I don’t know whether I can or not,” he muttered savagely. “Keep quiet for ten minutes, in the name of God.”

For a few dull seconds they paced in quiet. Patsy moistened his lips with his tongue.
===Chinese===
艾琳·尼·库利在他旁边走了一小段。

「你怎么了，帕德里克？」她说。

「没什么，艾琳，让我一个人待一会儿，我要跟这丫头说话。」

「有什么事你只管指望我，帕德里克。」

「指不指望得上我也不知道。」他凶巴巴地咕哝。「看在上帝份上，安静十分钟。」

有几秒钟他们闷闷地默默走着。帕齐用舌头润了润嘴唇。

===Original===
“What are you going to do, Mary?”

“I don’t know,” she replied. “What man did you sell the things to?”

“I sold them to a man that lives near by﻿—a rich man in a big house.”

“There’s only one big house about here.”

“That’s the house.”

She was silent.
===Chinese===
「你打算怎么办，玛丽？」

「我不知道。」她答道。「你把东西卖给了什么人？」

「卖给了一个住在近处的人——一个住在大宅子里的有钱人。」

「这一带只有一座大宅子。」

「就是那座宅子。」

她沉默了。

===Original===
“If you’re going to tell the men,” said her father, “give me two hours’ law this night until I get away, and then you can tell them and be damned to you.”

“Listen to me!” said the girl.

“I’m listening.”

“There is only one thing to be done, and it has got to be done at once: go you to the place of that rich man and take the things away from his house and bury them back again in the place they were buried. If you want any help I’ll go with you myself.”

Mac Cann’s thumb wandered to his chin and a sound as of filing was heard while he rubbed it. His voice was quite changed as he replied:

“Begor!” said he.
===Chinese===
「要是你打算告诉那几位，」她父亲说，「今晚给我两个钟头的宽限，让我跑远了，你再告诉他们去，随你遭殃。」

「听我说！」姑娘说。

「我听着。」

「只有一件事可做，而且必须马上就做：你到那个有钱人那里去，把东西从他家拿走，再埋回原先埋它们的地方。要人帮忙的话，我自己就跟你去。」

麦克·坎恩的大拇指又蹭到了下巴上，搓动时发出锉刀似的声音。他答话时嗓音全变了：

「老天爷！」他说。

===Original===
“You’re full of fun,” said he, thoughtfully. He covered his mouth with his hand then and stared thoughtfully down the road.

“Will you do that?” said Mary.

He thumped a hand heavily on her shoulder.

“I will so, and I do wonder that I didn’t think of it myself, for it’s the thing that ought to be done.”

And now as they marched the atmosphere had changed; there was once more peace or the precursor of it; from Mac Cann a tempered happiness radiated as of old: he looked abroad without misgiving and he looked at his daughter with the cynical kindliness habitual to him. They trod so for a little time arranging their thoughts, then:

“We are near enough to that house to be far enough from it if there’s any reason to be far,” said Mac Cann, “so this is what I say, let us stop where we are for the night and in the morning we’ll go on from here.”
===Chinese===
「你这丫头满肚子都是招儿。」他若有所思地说。随后他用手掩住嘴，一路沉思地望着路的前方。

「你肯去做吗？」玛丽说。

他把一只手沉沉地捶在她肩上。

「我肯！我还纳闷自己怎么没想到这一层，因为这事本来就该怎么着。」

这一走下去，气氛就变了；太平——或者说太平的先声——又回来了；麦克·坎恩身上重新散出那有分寸的快乐：他四下张望不再疑神疑鬼，他望女儿的眼神又是他惯常那副带几分讥诮的和气。他们这样走了一小段，各自理着心思，然后：

「咱们眼下离那座宅子，近得够办事，真要拉开距离也够远了，」麦克·坎恩说，「所以我说：就在这儿停下过夜，明早再从这儿上路。」

===Original===
“Very well,” said Mary, “let us stop here.”

Her father drew the ass to the side of the road and there halted it.

“We’ll go to bed now,” he shouted to the company, and they all agreed to that.

“I’m going to unyoke the beast,” said Mary with a steady eye on her father.

He replied heartily.

“Why wouldn’t you do that? Let him out to get something to eat like the rest of us.”
===Chinese===
「好，」玛丽说，「就在这儿停吧。」

她父亲把毛驴牵到路边，停了下来。

「咱们现在睡吧。」他朝众人喊道，大家全都没意见。

「我要卸驴套了。」玛丽说着，眼睛稳稳地盯着父亲。

他答得爽爽快快。

「怎么不该卸？放它也像咱们一样出去寻点吃的。」

===Original===
“There isn’t any water,” he complained a minute later. “What will that animal do? and what will we do ourselves?”

“I have two big bottles of water in the cart,” said Mary.

“And I have a little bottle in my pocket,” said he, “so we’re all right.”

The donkey was unyoked, and he went at once to stand with his feet in the wet grass. He remained so for a long time without eating, but he did eat when that idea occurred to him.

The brazier was lit, the sacks strewn on the ground, and they sat about the fire in their accustomed places and ate their food. After a smoke and a little conversation each person stretched backwards, covering themselves with other sacks, and they went heartily to sleep.

“We will have to be up early in the morning,” was Patsy’s last remark, “for you are in a hurry to get back your things,” and saying so he stretched his length with the others.

---

===Chinese===
「这儿没有水。」过了一分钟他抱怨道。「那牲口怎么办？咱们自己又怎么办？」

「车上有两大瓶水。」玛丽说。

「我口袋里还有一小瓶，」他说，「这样咱们就齐了。」

毛驴卸了套，立刻跑去站在湿漉漉的草地上。它就这么站了很久，什么也不吃；等吃这个念头自己冒出来的时候，它倒也吃了。

火盆点起来了，麻袋铺在地上，众人围着火坐在各自的老位置上吃了饭。抽了一阵烟、聊了几句之后，一个个向后躺倒，拿另外的麻袋往身上一盖，都酣酣地睡去了。

「明早咱们得早起，」这是帕齐最后一句话，「因为你们急着取回你们的东西。」说完他也跟大伙儿一样，伸直身子躺下了。

---

===Original===
When a still hour had drifted by Mary raised cautiously and tiptoed to her father. As she stood by him he slid the sacks aside and came to his feet, and they moved a little way down the road.

“Now,” said Mary, “you can do what you said you’d do.”

“I’ll do that,” said he.

“And get back as quick as you can.”

“It’s a distance there and back again. I’ll be here in the morning, but I’ll be late.”

“Bury the things the way they were before.”
===Chinese===
一个静默的钟头漂过去之后，玛丽小心地抬起身，踮着脚走到父亲跟前。她一站到他身边，他便把麻袋推开，站了起来，两人往路上挪开了一小段。

「好了，」玛丽说，「你可以去做你说过要做的事了。」

「我这就去。」他说。

「越快回来越好。」

「来回有一截路呢。早晨我会回来，但要晚些。」

「把东西照原样埋回去。」

===Original===
“That’s all right,” and he moved a step backwards.

“Father!” said Mary softly.

He returned to her.

“What more do you want?” said he impatiently.

She put her arms about his neck.

“What the devil are you doing?” said he in astonishment, and he tried to wriggle loose from her.
===Chinese===
「错不了。」他往后退了一步。

「爹！」玛丽轻轻唤了一声。

他又回到她跟前。

「你还有什么事？」他不耐烦地说。

她伸出双臂搂住了他的脖子。

「你这是搞什么名堂？」他惊愕地说，还挣扎着要从她怀里脱出身来。

===Original===
But she did not say another word, and after a moment he put his own arms about her with a grunt and held her tightly.

“I’m away now,” said he, and, moving against the darkness, he disappeared.

For half a minute the sound of his feet was heard, and then the darkness covered him.

Mary returned to her place by the brazier. She stretched close to Eileen Ni Cooley and lay staring at the moving clouds.

In a few minutes she was asleep, although she had not felt any heaviness on her eyes.

===Chinese===
可她再没说一个字，过了一会儿，他哼了一声，也伸出胳膊搂住她，搂得紧紧的。

「我走啦。」他说着，迎着黑暗走去，不见了。

有半分钟还能听见他的脚步声，随后黑暗就把他盖住了。

玛丽回到火盆边自己的位置上。她挨着艾琳·尼·库利躺下，仰望着流动的云。

不出几分钟她就睡着了，虽然她并不觉得眼皮有半分沉重。

### XXXII / 三十二

===Original===
No one was awake.

In the brazier a faint glow peeped from the white turf-ash; the earth seemed to be holding its breath, so still it was; the clouds hung immovably each in its place; a solitary tree near by folded its wide limbs into the darkness and made no sound.

Nothing stirred in the world but the ass as he lifted his head slowly and drooped it again; his feet were sunken in a plot of grass and he was quiet as the earth.

Then I came softly, and I spoke to the ass in the darkness.

“Little ass,” quoth I, “how is everything with you?”

“Everything is very well,” said the ass.
===Chinese===
没有人醒着。

火盆里，一线微光从白色的泥炭灰烬中探头；大地静得仿佛屏住了呼吸；云一朵一朵纹丝不动地悬在各自的位置上；近旁一棵孤树把它宽阔的枝臂拢进黑暗里，不发出一点声息。

世上万物都不动，只有那头毛驴缓缓抬起头，又垂下去；它的四脚陷在一小片草里，安静得像大地一样。

于是我轻轻走上前去，在黑暗里对毛驴说话。

「小毛驴，」我说道，「你一切都好吗？」

「一切都很好。」毛驴说。

===Original===
“Little ass,” said I, “tell me what you do be thinking of when you fix your eye on vacancy and stare there for a long time?”

“I do be thinking,” said the ass, “of my companions, and sometimes I do be looking at them.”

“Who are your companions?”

“Last night I saw the Cyclops striding across a hill; there were forty of them, and each man was forty feet high; they had only one eye in their heads and they looked through that; they looked through it the way a fire stares through a hole and they could see well.”

“How do you know they could see well?”

“One of them saw me and he called out to the others; they did not wait, but he waited for a moment; he took me in his arms and he stroked my head; then he put me on the ground and went away, and in ten strides he crossed over the mountain.”
===Chinese===
「小毛驴，」我说，「告诉我，你把眼睛盯在虚空上、久久地望在那里的时候，都在想些什么？」

「我想的是，」毛驴说，「我的那些同伴，有时候我还去看看它们。」

「你的同伴都是谁？」

「昨天夜里，我看见独眼巨人（the Cyclops）们大步跨过一座山；他们有四十个，每个都有四十英尺高；他们脑袋上只有一只眼睛，他们就透过那只眼睛看；看的样子就像一团火从洞里望出来，他们看得可真清楚。」

「你怎么知道他们看得清楚？」

「他们中间有一个看见了我，喊了其余的人；那些人没有等，他却等了一会儿；他把我抱在怀里，抚摸我的头；然后把我放到地上走开了，跨了十步就翻过了那座山。」

===Original===
“That was a good sight to see!”

“That was a good sight.”

“Tell me something else you saw.”

“I saw seven girls in a meadow and they were playing together; when they were tired playing they lay on the grass and they went to sleep; I drew near and stretched beside them on the grass, and I watched them for a long time; but when they awakened they disappeared into the air and were gone like puffs of smoke.

“I saw the fairy host marching through a valley in the hills; wide, silken banners were flying above their heads; some had long swords in their hands and some had musical instruments, and there were others who carried a golden apple in their hands, and others again with silver lilies and cups of heavy silver; they were beautiful and proud and they marched courageously; they marched past me for three gay hours while I stood on the slope of a hill.

“I saw three centaurs riding out of a wood; they raced round and round me shouting and waving their hands; one of them leaned his elbows on my back, and they talked of a place in the middle of a forest; they pelted me with tufts of grass; then they went by a narrow path into the wood, and they rode away.
===Chinese===
「那可真是好景致！」

「那是好景致。」

「再给我讲讲你见过的别的东西。」

「我见过七个姑娘在一片草地上一起玩耍；她们玩累了，就躺在草上睡着了；我凑近过去，在草地上挨着她们躺下，望了她们好久；可她们一醒来，就化进空气里不见了，像一缕缕烟。

「我见过仙灵军旅（the fairy host）穿过山中的一道山谷行军；宽阔的绸旗在他们头顶飞舞；有的人手里握着长剑，有的拿着乐器，还有的手里捧着一只金苹果，更有拿着银百合和沉甸甸的银杯的；他们美丽而骄傲，走得英勇豪迈；他们从我面前过了三个欢畅的钟头，我就站在一道山坡上。

「我见过三个半人马（centaurs）从一片树林里驰出来；他们围着我一圈圈地奔跑，又叫喊又挥手；其中一个把手肘支在我背上，他们谈论着森林正中的一个地方；他们拿一撮撮草团扔我；随后他们从一条窄路进了树林，驰走了。

===Original===
“I saw a herd of wild asses in a plain; men were creeping around them in the long grass, but the asses ran suddenly, and they killed the men with their hoofs and their teeth; I galloped in the middle of them for half a night, but I remembered Mary Ni Cahan, and when I remembered her I turned from all my companions and I galloped home again.”

“Those were all good sights to see!”

“They were all good sights.”

“Goodbye, little ass,” said I.

“Goodbye, you,” said he.

He lay along the grass then and he closed his eyes, but I turned back and crouched by the brazier, watching the people while they slept, and staring often into the darkness to see did anything stir before the light came.
===Chinese===
「我见过一群野驴在一片平原上；男人们在深草里朝他们潜行过去，可驴群突然奔跑起来，用蹄子和牙齿把那些人杀了；我在它们中间奔了半夜，可我想起了玛丽·尼·卡汉，一想起她，我就离开了所有的同伴，又奔跑着回家了。」

「这些全都是好景致！」

「全都是好景致。」

「再见，小毛驴。」我说。

「再见，你哪。」它说。

它随即顺着草躺下，闭上了眼睛；我却转身回去，蹲在火盆旁，守着沉睡的人们，时时望进黑暗里，看天亮之前可有什么东西在动。

### XXXIII / 三十三

===Original===
Mac Cann strode through the darkness for a little time, but when he found himself at sufficient distance from the camp he began to run.
There was not very much time wherein to do all that he had engaged before the morning dawned, and so he took to this mode of activity, which was not one for which he had any reverence. He was a heavy man and did not run with either grace or ease, but he could hasten his movements to a jog-trot, and, as his physical condition was perfect, he could continue such a trot until hunger brought it to a halt, for he was never fatigued, being as strong and tireless as a bear.
He was the most simple-minded of men. When he was engaged in one affair he could not meddle with anything else, and now that he was running he could do nothing but run﻿—he could not think, for instance. When it was necessary to think he would either walk very slowly or stand stock-still, and then he would think with great speed and with great simplicity. His head bade his legs be quiet while it was occupied, and, when they were in motion, his legs tramped hush to his head, which obeyed instantly; and he was so well organised on these lines that there was never any quarrel between the extremities.
===Chinese===
麦克·坎恩在黑暗里大步走了一阵，估摸着离营地够远了，便跑了起来。
天亮之前要把答应下的事全办完，时辰不多，于是他采用了这种活动方式——一种他素来不敬重的活动方式。他是个身量沉重的人，跑起来既不优雅也不轻快，但他能把步子加快成一路小跑，而且他身子骨结实得没话说，这样的小跑能一直跑到肚子饿才停，因为他从不疲倦，像熊一样壮实，一样不知疲累。
他是天下心眼最简单的人。手上一件事没了结，他就掺和不了旁的事，此刻既然在跑，他便只会跑——比如说，想是不能想的。到了非想不可的时候，他要么走得极慢，要么干脆钉在原地一动不动，然后又快又直截了当地想。脑子忙的时候，吩咐两腿安生；两腿动起来的时候，向脑袋喝一声“嘘”，脑袋立刻听命；他在这上头组织得如此之好，四肢之间从来没吵过架。

===Original===
It was, therefore, the emptiest of men that now pounded the road. He would deal with an emergency when it was visible, but until then he snapped a finger and forgot it, for he had learned that the first word of an emergency is a warning, the second a direction for escape, its third utterance is in action, and it will only be waited for by a fool.
Exactly what he would do when he arrived at the house he did not know, and as yet he made no effort to deal with that problem: he obeyed the prime logical necessity, which was to get there: once there and the second step would push itself against him, and from that cause the most orderly of results would ensue. If there was no trouble he would succeed in his enterprise; if there was trouble he would fly﻿—that was his simple programme.
And meantime there was nothing in the world but darkness and the rhythmic tramping of his feet. These, with a faintly hushing wind, kept his ears occupied. He had much of the cat’s facility for seeing in the dark, and he had the sense of direction which some birds have, so he made good progress.
After half an hour’s steady movement he came to the house for which he was seeking, and halted there.
===Chinese===
所以，此刻在路上踏得咚咚响的，是个心里最空的男人。急事到了眼面前他才去对付，在那之前，他打个响指就把它忘了，因为他早懂得：急事开口的第一句是警告，第二句是逃路，第三句出口时就该动手了，只有傻子才坐着等它。
到了那座房子究竟要干什么，他自己也不知道，眼下他也不费神去对付这个问题：他服从的是头一条逻辑上的必需，就是先到那儿：一到那儿，第二步自己就会撞上身来，顺理成章，结果自然井井有条。没麻烦，事情就成了；有麻烦，拔腿就飞——这就是他简单的章程。
这当口，世界上什么也没有，只有黑暗，和他双脚一板一眼的踏声。这些，加上一阵低低嘘着的夜风，把他的耳朵占满了。他颇有些猫在黑暗里看东西的本事，又有某些鸟才有的方向感，所以走得很快。
稳稳当当走了半个钟头，他到了要去的那座房子跟前，停了下来。

===Original===
It was a long, low building, standing back from the road. There was a stone wall around this house, and the entrance was by an iron gate.
Mac Cann touched the gate, for experience had taught him that gates are not always locked, but this one was locked securely. By the gate was a caretaker’s lodge, so he moved quietly from that place and walked by the wall.
There was glass on the top of the wall which halted him for a few moments while he sucked his incautious hand. To cope with this he gathered several large stones and placed them on top of each other and he stood on these, then he threw his coat and waistcoat over the glass and climbed easily across.
He was in a shrubbery. About him every few paces were short, stiff bushes, some of which were armed with spines, which did their duty on his hands and the legs of his trousers; but he regarded these with an inattention which must have disgusted them. He tiptoed among these guardians and was shortly free of them and on a gravel pathway. Crossing this he came on quiet flowerbeds, which he skirted: the house was now visible as a dark mass distant some hundred yards.
===Chinese===
那是一幢长而低矮的房子，退开大路立着。房子四周圈着石墙，进出走一道铁门。
麦克·坎恩碰了碰那门，经验教给他，门并不总是锁着的，可这扇门锁得严实。门旁有间看门人的小屋，他便悄悄挪开，沿着墙根走。
墙头上插着碎玻璃，拦了他一会儿，他吮着那只不小心被划了的手。对付这个，他搬来几块大石头一块叠一块，站上去，把外衣和马甲罩在玻璃碴上，轻轻松松翻了过去。
他落进了一片灌木丛。身边每隔几步就是一丛矮而硬的灌木，有几丛还长着刺，那些刺在他的手上、裤腿上恪尽职守；他却全不把它们放在眼里，想来很叫它们寒心。他踮着脚从这些卫士中间穿过去，不多时就脱了身，踏上一条砾石小径。穿过小径，眼前是一片安静的苗圃，他绕着走：这时那房子已经看得见了，黑沉沉一大团，离着约莫一百码。

===Original===
Saving for one window the place was entirely dark, and it was towards that window he directed his careful steps.
“It’s better to look at something than at nothing,” quoth he.
He was again on a gravel path, and the stones tried to crunch and wriggle under his feet, but he did not allow that to happen.
===Chinese===
整座宅子只有一扇窗亮着，他小心在意，直奔那扇窗去。
“瞧点东西总比瞧什么都不强。”他说。
他又踏上一段石子路，石子们想在他脚下咯吱作响、扭来扭去，但他没容它们得逞。

===Original===
He came to the window and, standing well to the side, peeped in.
He saw a square room furnished as a library. The entire section of the walls which he could spy was covered from floor to ceiling with books. There were volumes of every size, every shape, every colour. There were long, narrow books that held themselves like grenadiers at stiff attention. There were short, fat books that stood solidly like aldermen who were going to make speeches and were ashamed but not frightened. There were mediocre books bearing themselves with the carelessness of folk who are never looked at and have consequently no shyness. There were solemn books that seemed to be feeling for their spectacles; and there were tattered, important books that had got dirty because they took snuff, and were tattered because they had been crossed in love and had never married afterwards. There were prim, ancient tomes that were certainly ashamed of their heroines and utterly unable to obtain a divorce from the hussies; and there were lean, rakish volumes that leaned carelessly, or perhaps it was with studied elegance, against their neighbours, murmuring in affected tones, “All heroines are charming to us.”
In the centre of the room was a heavy, black table, and upon the highly polished surface of this a yellow light fell from globes on the ceiling.
===Chinese===
他走到窗前，远远偏在一边，朝里偷看。
他看见一间方形屋子，布置成书房的样子。他望得见的那一面墙，从地板到天花板满满全是书。什么开本、什么形状、什么颜色的书都有。有又长又窄的书，立得像挺直了军姿的掷弹兵。有又矮又胖的书，敦敦实实站着，像正要登台演讲的市参议员，害臊归害臊，倒不害怕。有平平庸庸的书，一副满不在乎的做派，像那些从没人看第二眼、因此也从不腼腆的人。有板着脸的书，像正在摸索自己的眼镜；还有破破烂烂的大部头，因为爱闻鼻烟所以脏了，因为情场失意所以破了，后来也终身未娶。有古板的老学术书，分明是为自家的女主角们害臊，却又怎么也没法同那些轻狂女人离掉；还有瘦长风流的书卷，懒懒地——也许是刻意优雅地——倚着邻居，拿腔拿调地嘀咕：“天下的女主角，在我们眼里个个可爱。”
屋子正中是一张厚重的黑桌子，天花板上的球形灯把一汪黄光泻在擦得锃亮的桌面上。

===Original===
At this table a man was seated, and he was staring at his hands. He was a man of about thirty years of age. A tall, slender man with a lean face, and, to Patsy, he was of an appalling cleanliness﻿—a cleanliness really to make one shudder: he was shaved to the last closeness; he was washed to the ultimate rub; on him both soap and water had wrought their utmost, and could have no further ambitions; his wristlets gleamed like snow on a tree, and his collar rose upon a black coat as the plumage of a swan emerges spotlessly from water.
His cleanliness was a sight to terrify any tramp, but it only angered Mac Cann, who was not liable to terror of anything but hunger.
“I would like to give you a thump on the head, you dirty dog!” said Patsy, breathing fiercely against the corner of the windowpane, and his use of the adjective was singular as showing in what strange ways extremes can meet.
This was the man to whom he had sold the gear of his companions: an indelicate business indeed, and one which the cleanliness of the purchaser assisted him to rectify, and it was in this room that the barter had been conducted. By craning his neck a little he could see an oaken settle, and upon this his sacks were lying with their mouths open and the gleaming cloths flooding at the entry.
===Chinese===
桌边坐着一个男人，正盯着自己那双手。他年纪三十上下，又高又瘦，一张清瘦的脸，而在帕齐眼里，他干净得吓人——那种干净真能叫人打个寒噤：胡子刮到了根；澡搓到了底；肥皂和清水在他身上已经尽了全力，再不能有更高的志向；他的袖口亮得像树上落雪，领子从黑礼服上竖起，如天鹅的羽毛从水里钻出来，一尘不染。
他这份干净，哪个流浪汉见了都要胆寒，可它只是惹恼了麦克·坎恩——他这人除了饥饿，什么都不怕。
“真想照你脑袋给一下，你这脏狗！”帕齐说，朝窗玻璃的角上狠狠呵着气。他这形容词用得别致，可见两个极端能在多么古怪的地方碰头。
这就是他把自己同伴那身行头卖给的这个人：那桩买卖实在有欠厚道，而买主的这份干净正好帮他来纠正它；那场交易就是在这间屋里做成的。他把脖子略略一伸，就能看见一张橡木高背长椅，他的两条口袋正摊在椅上，张着口，闪亮的布料从袋口涌流出来。

===Original===
While he stared, the man removed his fingers from his eyes and put them in his pocket, then he arose very slowly and paced thoughtfully towards the window.
Mac Cann immediately ducked beneath the window ledge. He heard the window opened and knew the man was leaning his elbows on the sill while he stared into the darkness.
“Begor!” said Patsy to himself, and he flattened his body against the wall.
After a time, which felt longer than it could have been, he heard the man moving away, and he then popped up and again peeped through the window.
===Chinese===
他正盯着，那人把手指从眼睛上挪开，插进口袋，随后极慢地站起身，若有所思地朝窗子踱来。
麦克·坎恩立刻矮身躲到窗台底下。他听见窗子打开，知道那人正把胳膊肘支在窗台上，望着外头的黑暗出神。
“乖乖！”帕齐心里说，把身子贴平在墙上。
过了一阵——觉得比实际的要长——他听见那人走开了，便又探起身，凑到窗上往里瞧。

===Original===
The man had opened the door of the room which faced the window and was standing in the entry. Now his hands were clasped behind his back, his head was sunken forward, and he seemed to be looking at his feet, which is the habit of many men when they think, for when the eyes touch the feet a circuit is formed and one’s entire body is able to think at ease.
Suddenly the man stepped into a black corridor and he disappeared. Mac Cann heard about ten steps ringing from a solid flooring, then he heard a door open and shut, then he heard nothing but the shifting and rubbing of his own clothes and the sound his own nose made when he breathed outwards: there was a leathern belt about his middle, and from the noise which it made one would have fancied that it was woven of thunders﻿—there was a great silence; the lighted room was both inviting and terrifying, for it was even more silent than the world outside; the steady globes stared at the window like the eyes of a mad fish, and one could imagine that the room had pricked up invisible ears and was listening towards the window, and one could imagine also that the room would squeak and wail if any person were to come through anywhere but a door and stand in it.
Mac Cann did not imagine any of these things. He spat on his hands, and in the twinkling of an eye he was inside the window. In three long and hasty paces he placed a hand on each of the sacks, and just as he gripped them he heard a door opening, and he heard the footsteps ringing again on a solid flooring.
“I’m in,” said he, viciously, “and I won’t go out.”
===Chinese===
那人已推开正对窗子的房门，站在门洞里。这会儿他双手背在身后，头往前一沉，像是在看自己的脚——许多人想事情时都有这习惯，因为眼睛一落到脚上，回路就接通了，浑身上下都能安安逸逸地思想。
突然，那人跨进一条黑走廊，不见了。麦克·坎恩听见结实的地板上响起约莫十步脚步声，接着听见一扇门开了又关上，再往后，就只剩下自己衣裳的窸窣摩擦声，和鼻子往外呼气时的声音了：他腰里扎根皮带，听它那一阵阵的动静，人家准以为它是用雷声织成的——四下里一片大寂静；那间亮着灯的屋子既叫人向往又叫人发怵，因为它比外头的世界还要静；纹丝不动的球形灯瞪着窗子，像疯鱼的眼睛；你能想象那屋子竖起了看不见的耳朵，正朝着窗子这边听；你还能想象，要是有人不从门、偏从别处钻进来站到屋里，那屋子准会吱吱哇哇地叫起来。
这些麦克·坎恩一样也没想。他朝手心啐了口唾沫，眨眼间已经翻进了窗子。三大步又急又长，他一手按住一条口袋，恰恰在他攥住它们的那一刻，听见一扇门开了，脚步声又在结实的地板上响起来。
“我进来了，”他狠狠地说，“就不打算出去了。”

===Original===
His eyes blinked around like the flash of lightning but there was no place to hide. He stepped across the oaken chest and crouched down. Behind him, from the floor upwards, were books, in front was the big chest, and on top of it the two bulging sacks. He was well screened and he could peep between the sacks.
He stared towards the door.
The clean man came in and stood aside. Following him came a woman who was, if anything, more rigorously washed than he was. Somehow, although she was a tall woman, she seemed as light as a feather. She was clad in a delicate pink gown of such gossamer quality that it balanced and swam on the air with every movement she made. Across her bare shoulders was a lawn veiling, which also sailed and billowed as she moved. Her hair seemed to be of the finest spun gold, light as thistledown, and it, too, waved and floated in little strands and ringlets.
These two people sat down at different sides of the table, and for a time they did not speak to each other. Then the man raised his head:
===Chinese===
他的眼睛闪电般四下一扫，却没有藏身的地方。他跨过那橡木柜，蹲了下去。身后从地板往上全是书，前头是大柜子，柜顶上搁着两条鼓鼓囊囊的口袋。遮得严实，他还能从两条口袋的缝里往外瞧。
他盯着门口。
那个干净人走了进来，闪身站在一边。跟着进来一个女人，若说有什么不同，就是洗涮得比他还彻底。也不知怎么，她分明是个高个儿女人，看上去却轻得像根羽毛。她穿一身娇嫩的粉红长裙，纱薄得像蛛丝，她每动一动，那裙子便在空气里浮摆游漾。裸露的双肩上搭一袭细纱披纱，也随着她的动作鼓荡飘扬。她的头发像是极细的纺金，轻若蓟花冠毛，也一绺绺、一卷卷地波荡漂浮。
这两人在桌子的两边各自坐下，有一阵子谁也不理谁。随后那男人抬起头来：

===Original===
“I got a letter from your mother this morning,” said he in a low voice.
The woman answered him in a tone that was equally low:
“I did not know you corresponded with her.”
The man made a slight gesture:
“Nor did I know that your correspondence was as peculiar as I have found it,” said he.
===Chinese===
“今天早晨我收到你母亲一封信。”他低声说。
那女人用同样低的声音答他：
“我原先不知道你同她有书信来往。”
男人微微做了个手势：
“我也不知道你的通信竟有我发现的那样特别。”他说。

===Original===
Said the woman coldly:
“You are opening this subject again.”
“I am: I have to: your mother confirms everything that I have charged you with.”
“My mother hates me,” said the woman, “she would confirm anything that was said of me, if it was bad enough.”
“She is your mother.”
===Chinese===
那女人冷冷地说：
“你又提起这个话头了。”
“是又提了：不提不行：你母亲把我指控你的每一件事都坐实了。”
“我母亲恨我，”那女人说，“只要话说得够狠，人家说我什么她都肯坐实。”
“她是你母亲。”

===Original===
“Oh no, she is not! When I ceased to be a child she ceased to be a mother. We are only two women who are so well acquainted that we can be enemies without any shame of each other.”
“Are you not talking nonsense?”
“I have committed a crime against her. She will never forgive me for being younger than she is, and for being pretty in her own fashion. She left my father because he said I was good-looking.”
“All that﻿ ﻿… !” said the man with a movement of his shoulder.
“As to what she would do against me, you should know it well enough considering the things she told you before we were married.”
===Chinese===
“哦不，她不是！我一不再是孩子，她就不再是母亲。我们不过是两个女人，彼此熟到能做仇人，谁也不必为谁害臊。”
“你这不是在胡说吗？”
“我对她犯过一桩罪。她永远不能原谅我比她年轻，不能原谅我生了我自己那一份的好看。我父亲就因为说我长得俊，被她甩了。”
“这些都是……！”男人说着，肩膀动了一下。
“至于她会怎么对付我，你该再清楚不过了，想想咱们结婚前她跟你说过的那些话。”
===Original===
“You admitted that they were not all lies.”
“Some of the facts were true, all of the colouring was false﻿—they are the things a loving mother says about her daughter! but that is an old story now, or I had fancied so.”
“One forgets the old story until the new story drags it to memory,” said he.
She also moved her shoulders slightly.
“I begin to find these conversations tiresome.”
===Chinese===
“你承认过，那些话不全是瞎编。”
“有几件事实是真的，可所有的渲染全是假的——那就是一位慈母说自家女儿的话！不过这是老黄历了，或者我原以为是。”
“人会把老黄历忘了，直到新故事把它拽回记忆里来。”他说。
她也微微耸了耸肩。
“我开始觉得这些谈话叫人厌烦了。”

===Original===
“I can understand that﻿ ﻿… With her letter your mother enclosed some other letters from her friends﻿—they insist on the facts, and add others.”
“Are they letters, or copies of letters?”
“They are copies.”
“Of course my mother has forbidden you to disclose the fact that she forwarded her friends’ private correspondence to you.”
“Naturally.”
===Chinese===
“这我明白……你母亲随信还附了她几位朋友写来的信——那些信咬死了事实，还添了些新的。”
“是信呢，还是信的抄本？”
“是抄本。”
“我母亲自然禁止你说出她把朋友们的私信转寄给你这件事。”
“自然。”

===Original===
“Very naturally; the reason being that she wrote these letters herself to herself. There are no originals of these copies.”
“Again you are talking nonsense.”
“I know her better than you do, better than she knows herself.”
There was silence between them again for a few moments, and again it was broken by the man.
“There are some things I cannot do,” said he, and paused:
===Chinese===
“自然之至；缘由嘛，是那些信本来就是她自己写给自己的。这些抄本压根没有原件。”
“你又在胡说了。”
“我比你看她看得透，比她自己看自己还透。”
两人之间又静了片刻，又还是那男人先开的口。
“有些事情我做不来。”他说，顿了顿：

===Original===
“I cannot search in unclean places for unclean information,” he continued, and again the silence fell between these two people.
She could bear that silence, but he could not:
“You do not say anything!” said he.
“This seems to be so entirely your business,” was her quiet reply.
He moved a hand at that:
===Chinese===
“我不能到不干净的地方去挖不干净的消息。”他接着说，寂静又落在这两个人之间。
那份寂静她受得住，他受不住：
“你怎么一句话也不说！”他说。
“这事看样子整个儿是你自己的事。”她安安静静地答道。
他听了这话挥了一下手：

===Original===
“You cannot divorce yourself from me with such ease. This is our business, and we must settle it between us.”
Her hand was resting on the table, and suddenly he reached to her and laid his own hand on hers. She did not withdraw, but the stiffening of her body was more than withdrawal. He drew his hand away again.
“We are reasonable creatures and must question our difficulties,” said he gently, “we must even help each other to resolve them.”
“These difficulties are not of my making.”
“They are, and you are lying to me shamelessly.”
===Chinese===
“你不能这么轻巧地就把自己从我这里摘出去。这是我们两个人的事，得由我们俩当面解决。”
她的一只手正搁在桌上，他忽然探过身去，把手搭在她的手上。她没有抽回，可她身子一僵，比抽回还意味深长。他又把手收了回来。
“我们是有理性的人，遇到的难题就得摆开来问，”他温声说，“甚至还得帮着对方一道解开。”
“这些难题不是我造出来的。”
“就是你造的，你还不知羞耻地对我撒谎。”

===Original===
Again between these people a silence fell which was profound but not quiet. That soundlessness was tingling with sound; there were screams latent in it; it was atrocious and terrifying. The man’s hand was pressed against his forehead and his eyes were closed, but what he was looking at was known only to himself in the silence of his being. The woman sat upright an arm’s length from him, and although her eyes were wide and calm, she also was regarding that which was free within herself, and very visible to her.
“There are things I cannot do,” said the man, emerging as with an effort from subterranean caves and secret prospects. He continued speaking, calmly but tonelessly:
“I have striven to make a rule of life for myself and to follow it, but I have not sought to impose my laws on anyone else﻿—not on you, certainly. Still there are elementary duties which we owe to one another and which cannot be renounced by either of us. There is a personal, I might say, a domestic loyalty expected by each of us.﻿ ﻿…”
“I expect nothing,” said she.
“I exact nothing,” said the man, “but I expect that﻿—I expect it as I expect air for my lungs and stability under my feet. You must not withdraw that from me. You are not the individual you think; you are a member of society, and you live by it; you are a member of my household, and you live by it.”
===Chinese===
这两个人之间又落下一阵寂静，深得很，却不安静。那无声里嗡嗡全是声响；尖叫声潜伏在其中；它凶残，吓人。男人一只手抵着额头，闭着眼睛，但他望着什么，只有他自己在他自身的寂静里知道。那女人在一臂开外坐得笔直，她眼睛虽然睁得大大的、很平静，她也在望着自己心里那自由放任的东西，而且看得一清二楚。
“有些事情我做不来。”那男人说，像费了番力气才从地底洞穴和幽秘的景象里钻出来。他接着往下讲，平静，却没有一点起伏：
“我努力给自己立下一条生活的规矩，并且遵守它，可我并没有想把我的法则强加给旁人——当然也没有强加给你。但总有些彼此相欠的基本道义，你我谁也不能推卸。我们每个人，都指望对方有一份个人的——我可以说是家内的——忠贞。……”
“我什么都不指望。”她说。
“我什么也不强求，”那男人说，“但我指望这个——指望它，就像指望肺里有空气、脚下有踏实的地。你不能把这个也从我这儿抽走。你不是你自以为的那个人；你是社会的一员，靠社会活着；你是我这个家的一员，靠这个家活着。”

===Original===
She turned her face to him but not her eyes.
“I do not ask anything from you,” said she, “and I have accepted as little as was possible.”
He clenched his hand on the table, but when he spoke his voice was without emphasis:
“That is part of my grievance against you. Life is to give and take without any weighing of the gifts. You will do neither, and yet our circumstances are such that we must accommodate each other whether we will or not.”
“I am an exact man,” he continued, “perhaps you find that trying, but I cannot live in doubt. Whatever happens to hinder or assist my consciousness must be known to me. It is a law of my being: it is my ancestral heritage, and I have no command over it.”
===Chinese===
她把脸转向他，眼睛却没有。
“我不问你要任何东西，”她说，“能少接受的，我也已经少接受到了不能再少。”
他把按在桌上的手攥紧了，可开口时，话音里听不出轻重：
“这也是我对你的怨气之一。生活就是给予和接受，不必掂量来掂量去。你两样都不肯，可咱们的处境摆在这儿，不管愿不愿意，都必须彼此迁就。”
“我是个事事要求确切的人，”他接着说，“也许你觉得这很难受，可我没办法活在疑惑里。凡是妨碍或助成我心神的事，我必须知道得一清二楚。这是我生命的一条法则：是祖上传下来的家产，我做不了它的主。”

===Original===
“I also,” said she coldly, “am an heir of the ages, and must take my bequests whether I like them or not.”
“I love you,” said the man, “and I have proved it many times. I am not demonstrative, and I am shy of this fashion of speech. Perhaps that shyness of speech is responsible for more than is apparent to either of us in a world eager for speech and gesture, but I say the word now in all sincerity, with a gravity, perhaps, which you find repulsive. Be at least as honest with me, no matter how cruel you are. I cannot live in the half-knowledge which is jealousy. It tears my heart. It makes me unfit for thought, for life, for sleep, even for death. I must know, or I am a madman and no man any longer, a wild beast that will bite itself in despair of hurting its enemy.”
The woman’s tongue slipped over her pale lips in a quick, red flash.
“Have you anything to say to me?” said he.
There was no reply.
===Chinese===
“我，”她冷冷地说，“也是历代的继承人，不管喜欢不喜欢，名下那份遗产也得照单全收。”
“我爱你，”那男人说，“这一点我证明过许多回。我不惯于外露，这样子说话我臊得慌。在一个人人急着言语和手势的世上，这份言语上的羞怯，也许要为许多事情负责，多得咱们俩谁都看不出来；可这句话我如今说得诚心诚意，也许还带着一种你觉得可憎的郑重。那你至少也对我诚实些，随你多狠。我活不成那种半知半解的日子，半知半解就是嫉妒。它撕我的心。它叫我配不上思想，配不上生活，配不上睡眠，甚至配不上死。我必须知道，不然我就是个疯子，不再是一个人，一头伤不着仇家、绝望得只能咬自己的野兽。”
那女人的舌头在苍白的唇上一掠，闪过一线迅疾的红。
“你有什么话要对我说吗？”他说。
没有回答。

===Original===
He insisted:
“Are the statements in your mother’s letter true?”
“My mother’s letter!” said she.
“Have I reason for this jealousy?” he breathed.
Her reply was also but a breathing:
===Chinese===
他追问：
“你母亲信里说的那些话，是不是真的？”
“我母亲的信！”她说。
“我这嫉妒，可有来由？”他低声道。
她的回答也只是一口气息：

===Original===
“I will not tell you anything,” said she.
Once again the silence drowsed and droned between the two people, and again they repaired to the secret places of their souls where energy was sucked from them until they existed only in a torpor. The woman rose languidly from her chair, and, after an instant, the man stood also.
Said he:
“I will leave here in the morning.”
“You will let me see the boy,” she murmured.
===Chinese===
“我什么都不会告诉你。”她说。
寂静又一次在这两个人之间昏昏嗡嗡地漫开，他们又退回各自灵魂的密室里去，力气在那儿被一点点吸走，直到只剩下麻木地存在着。那女人恹恹地从椅子上站起身；片刻之后，男人也站了起来。
他说：
“我明早就离开这儿。”
“你要让我见见孩子。”她低声说。

===Original===
“If,” said he, “I ever learn that you have spoken to the boy I will kill you, and I will kill the boy.”
The woman went out then, and her feet tapped lightly along the corridor. The man turned down the lights in the yellow globes and stepped to the door; his footsteps also died away in the darkness, but in a different direction.
---
===Chinese===
“要是，”他说，“让我知道你跟那孩子说过一个字，我就杀了你，也杀了那孩子。”
那女人随即出去了，脚步沿着走廊轻轻点去。男人捻暗了黄球灯，走向房门；他的脚步声也在黑暗里消逝了，只是方向不同。
---

===Original===
Mac Cann stood up:
“Begor!” said he, stretching his cramped knees.
About him was a great darkness and a great silence, and the air of that room was more unpleasant than any atmosphere he had ever breathed. But he had the nerves of a bear and a resolute adherence to his own business, so the excitement of another person could only disturb him for a moment. Still, he did not like the room, and he made all haste to get out of it.
He lifted the sacks, stepped carefully to the window, and dropped them out. Then he climbed through and picked them up.
In five minutes he was on the road again. Along it for some dozen yards he trod like a great cat until he had left the gatekeeper’s lodge well behind him; then, with the sacks across his shoulders, he shook to the steady jog trot which was to last for about three hours.
===Chinese===
麦克·坎恩站起身：
“乖乖！”他说，伸展着蹲麻了的膝盖。
他四周是大大的黑暗和大大的寂静，那屋里的空气，比他呼吸过的任何空气都叫他不受用。可他有熊一样的神经，又咬定自己的事不放松，别人家的情潮只能搅扰他一小会儿。话虽如此，他到底不喜欢这间屋子，便火速想法子出去了。
他提起两条口袋，小心走到窗前，把它们扔了出去。随后他自己翻出窗子，把它们拾起来。
五分钟后他又上了大路。头十来码他像头大猫一样轻踏着走，直到把看门人的小屋远远甩在身后；然后，两条口袋往肩上一扛，他抖擞精神，踏上了要持续约莫三个钟头的那一路稳稳的小跑。

### XXXIV / 三十四

===Original===
Mary awakened early.

The morning was grey and the sky flat and solid, with here and there thin furrows marking its gathered fields.

She raised her head, and looked towards her father’s place, but he was not there, and the sacks were crumpled on the ground.

Finaun’s great length was lying along the ground, and he was straight as a rod. Caeltia was curved a little, and one hand was flung above his head. Art was rolled up like a ball; his hands were gripped about his knees, and he had kicked the sacks off his body. Eileen Ni Cooley had her two arms under her face; she was lying on her breast, and her hair streamed sidewards from her head along the dull grass.

As Mary lay back, for it was still too early to rise, a thought came to her and she rose to her feet again. She thought that perhaps her father had come softly in the night and moved the ass and cart away with him, and that thought lifted her breast in panic.

===Chinese===
玛丽醒得很早。

清晨灰蒙蒙的，天空又平又实，间或有一道道细纹，标出它拢起的田垄。

她抬起头，朝她父亲睡的地方望去，人却不在，麻袋皱作一团摊在地上。

菲瑙恩长长的身子直挺挺躺在地上，直得像一根杆子。凯尔提亚微微蜷着，一只手甩在头顶上。阿特蜷成一团像个球；双手抱紧膝盖，麻袋早叫他踢下了身。艾琳·尼·库利两条手臂垫在脸下；她伏着胸躺，头发从头顶向一侧流淌，漫过暗沉沉的草地。

玛丽重新躺下——因为起身还太早——一个念头忽然袭来，她又一骨碌站了起来。她想到，兴许父亲夜里蹑手蹑脚回来，牵上毛驴、拉上大篷车独自走了，这念头吓得她心口猛地一掀。

===Original===
She ran down the road and saw the cart with its shafts poked in the air, and further away the donkey was lying on his side.

She came back on tiptoe smiling happily to herself, and, with infinite precaution, she restored the sacks to Art’s body and composed herself again to sleep. She did not raise the camp, for she wished to give her father all possible time so that he might return unnoticed.

And while she slept the sky unpacked its locked courses; the great galleons of cloud went sailing to the west, and thus, fleet by fleet, relieved those crowded harbours. The black cloud-masses went rolling on the sky﻿—They grew together, touched and swung apart and slipped away with heavy haste, as when down narrow waters an armada weighs, filling listlessly her noisy sails, while the slender spars are hauled to the breeze; the watchmen stand at the posts, and the fenders are still hung from the pitching sides; almost the vessels touch; the shipmen shout as they bear heavily on their oaken poles; and then they swing again, the great prows bear away, the waters boil between, and the loud farewells sing faintly to the waves.

And now the sky was a bright sea sown with islands; they shrank and crumbled and drifted away, islands no more, but a multitude of plumes and flakes and smoky wreaths hastily scudding, for the sun had lifted his tranquil eye on the heavens; he stared afar down the grey spaces, and before his gaze the mists went huddling and hiding in lovely haste; the dark spaces became white, the dark blue spaces became light blue, and earth and sky sparkled and shone in his radiant beam.

---

===Chinese===
她顺着大路跑去，看见大篷车好端端停着，车杆指向天上，再远一点，毛驴侧身卧着。

她踮着脚尖回来，自顾自美滋滋地笑着，又以万分的谨慎把麻袋重新盖回阿特身上，然后安顿自己接着睡。她没有惊动营里的人，因为她想给父亲留出一切可能的时间，好让他神不知鬼不觉地回来。

她睡着的时候，天空卸开了它锁闭的航道；云的大帆船一艘艘向西航行，就这样一支接一支舰队地，疏散了那些拥挤的港汊。黑色的云团在天上滚滚而行——它们聚拢、相触、又荡开，带着沉重的匆忙滑走，恰似一支大舰队起锚驶下狭窄的水面，懒懒地张满喧哗的帆，细长的桅杆被拉向微风；瞭望的人各守其位，护舷还挂在颠簸的船帮上；船与船几乎相擦；水手们抵着橡木篙，吃力地吆喝；随后它们再度荡开，巨大的船头转向驶去，中间的水沸腾起来，响亮的道别声向波涛隐隐唱去。

这时天空成了一片撒满岛屿的明亮海洋；岛屿萎缩、碎散、漂走了，不再是岛屿，化作无数羽翎、薄片和烟雾的花环匆匆掠过，因为太阳已把他安宁的眼睛抬向高天；他朝灰蒙蒙的远处深深凝望，在他的注视下，雾气可爱的慌忙里挤挤挨挨地躲藏起来；暗处变白了，深蓝处变成浅蓝，大地和天空都在他灿烂的光芒里熠熠生辉。

---

===Original===
The camp awakened before Mary did, and again the enquiry went as to the whereabouts of her father:

“He will be here shortly,” said Mary. “He must have gone along the road to see if there was anything he could find for us to eat,” and she delayed the preparation of their breakfast to the last possible moment. She spilled a pot of boiling water to that end, and she overturned the brazier when the water boiled again.

They were about sitting to their food when Mac Cann came in sight, and she held the meal until his arrival with his hat far to the back of his head, the happiest of smiles on his face, and a newspaper bundle in his hand.

Mary gave him a look of quick meaning:

“Were you able to find anything for the breakfast?” said she, and then she was astonished.

===Chinese===
营里的人比玛丽先醒，于是又问起她父亲的下落：

“他很快就回来了，”玛丽说，“他准是顺着大路去了，看看能不能给咱们找点什么吃的，”于是她把早饭的准备工作拖到了最后一刻。为着这个，她打翻了一壶滚水，等水再烧开时，又把火盆弄翻了。

他们正要坐下开饭，麦克·坎恩出现在眼前，她便把饭压着，一直等到他到来——帽子远远推向脑后，脸上挂着天下最开心的笑，手里拎着一个报纸包。

玛丽飞快地递给他一个意味深长的眼色：

“早饭你找到什么了吗？”她说，接着便吃了一惊。

===Original===
“I was indeed,” he replied, and he handed her the bulky newspaper package.

She used that occasion to whisper to him:

“Well?”

“That’s all right,” said he, nodding at the bundle, but really in answer to her query.

She opened the parcel.

===Chinese===
“还真找着了，”他答道，把那个鼓鼓囊囊的报纸包递给她。

她趁这个机会凑到他耳边低声问：

“怎么样？”

“妥了，”他说，一边朝纸包点头，其实是回答她的问话。

她打开纸包。

===Original===
There were slices of bacon in it and slices of beef; there were ten sausages in it and the biggest half of a loaf﻿—these, with a small flat bottle full of rum and two pairs of stockings, made up the parcel.

“Put the sausages in a pan,” said Patsy, “and share them round and we’ll eat them.”

Mary did put them on the pan, and when they were cooked she shared them round, and they were fairly eaten.

After breakfast the pipes were lit, but they rose almost immediately to continue the journey.

“This evening,” said Finaun, “we will be saying goodbye.”

===Chinese===
里头有培根片，有牛肉片；有十根香肠，还有大半个面包——再加上一小扁瓶满溢的朗姆酒和两双长袜，这纸包就齐了。

“把香肠下锅，”帕齐说，“分给大家，咱们吃掉。”

玛丽果真把香肠下了锅，煎熟后分给众人，大家吃得干干净净。

饭后点起了烟斗，可他们几乎马上就起身赶路了。

“今天傍晚，”菲瑙恩说，“我们就要道别了。”

===Original===
“Aye,” said Mac Cann, “I’m sorry you’re going, for we had a good time together.”

The ass took his instructions, and they went down the road.
